# Emend — Engineering Journal

> **Renamed 2026-11-08:** the app was Cowrite until D36 and TD-29. Entries dated before 2026-11-08 keep the old name, the old on-disk names (`.cowrite/`, `COWRITE_*`, `com.cowrite.desktop`) and bare `#NNNN` issue numbers, which refer to `williacj/cowrite`. They are left as written, because they record what was true then. Links to cowrite issues, PRs and runs were made relative by CJ's ruling of 2026-11-10 (#12), so they now resolve within `williacj/emend`.

# 2026-11-11 — williacj/emend#62 forked Rigger prompts adapted to the constitution

- **Technical Insights.** The verdict marker's format never lived in the forked prompts. Rigger's engine writes the marker instruction into each judge's dispatch (`src/workflow/marker.mjs`, `markerInstruction`), and the gate parses the same schema. What the prompts must keep is the contract around it: the three verdicts (`sound`, `needs revision`, `critical`), a met/unmet ruling on every acceptance item, the acceptance-coverage ruling, what would make a non-sound verdict sound, and binding to one head and one acceptance revision. `reviewer.md`'s "What you return" is unchanged, and code-review's "What you return" and "The verdict" lose only their `R-*` ids.
- **Register mapping.** Rigger's `docs/spec/requirements.md` (R-*) maps to `docs/functional-spec.md` (FR-*, with product decisions as D#). Its `docs/spec/decisions.md` maps to the decision logs of `docs/functional-spec.md` and `docs/tech-design.md` (TD-#). `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s layers map to `docs/tech-design.md` §3.1. Rigger has no counterpart to `docs/ux-design.md` (J#), `docs/test-plan.md` (P1–P6) or `docs/design/`, so the code-review lenses are now one per constitution document plus CLAUDE.md's invariants. The R-* group lenses, `README.md`'s verb contract and `docs/v0-build-plan.md` are gone.
- **Rigger bars, as CJ ruled on PR #68 (2026-11-11).** Not adopted: mutation and `proof:diff` receipts, `// proves` behaviour-deletion probes, Rigger's tdd sections "A test a change adds" and "A mutation claim", the test-line grant protocol, line budgets, the fault-class sweep, retained originals, contract-state maps and limit/dimension proposal rows. These are removed outright, leaving no marker text. Deferred until the canary shows a need: the pre-dispatch acceptance attack. Adopted: "Documents own their facts", as a short AGENTS.md rule (in #16's branch), which code-review, acceptance, agent-style and spec-style now cite. Spec-style keeps Rigger's rules 1, 3 and 4, renumbered 1–3, and drops the 25/40-word ceiling.
- **Second review and rulings (2026-11-11).** The architect now also makes `type:tech-design` cards, and the PM and proposal skill send a TD-# delta to one. A dispatched PM escalates mock-implied behaviour (as `recorded-decision` where it would change a recorded J#, otherwise `ambiguous`) instead of handing it to a Designer, which is not a configured role. Code-review judges tests against the tdd skill's "Make the assertion discriminate" rather than running a skill whose description excludes independent review, and checks for hand edits under `docs/design/`. Leftover vocabulary from the rejected bars ("cell", proof-credit classes, "unresolved attempts", mutation probes) is reworded in Emend's terms, and the acceptance example now traces to FR-ED-1.
- **`rigger doctor` config line.** In this worktree it fails only on `board.project: 'PROJECT_NUMBER'` (#14). On a scratch copy with a numeric board, `config validity` reports "earns no refusals", so the adapted prompt and skill paths satisfy the validator.

# 2026-11-11 — williacj/emend#16 CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md point at Rigger

- **Architectural Decisions.** CLAUDE.md keeps Emend's project rules and gains "Cards and Rigger": how a card is made, judged, merged and escalated, with Rigger's guide and `rigger.config.mjs` as the owners of the detail. AGENTS.md now holds the rules that process puts on every session: "Sessions and roles", "Review and merge", "Keeping hand work and the engine apart", "Git operations reserved to CJ" and "Version control". CLAUDE.md imports AGENTS.md (`@AGENTS.md`), because Claude Code loads only CLAUDE.md by itself and the forked hook's denial text sends a session to AGENTS.md.
- **The reserved operations are what the hooks refuse, written as the rule.** `.claude/hooks/refuse-reserved-git-commands.mjs` refuses force pushes (`-f`, `--force`, `--force-with-lease`, `--force-if-includes`, `+refspec`), branch deletion (`branch -d/-D/--delete`, `push --delete/-d`, `:branch`), `commit -n`/`--no-verify`, `push --no-verify`, `-c core.hooksPath=…`, and a literal push to the default branch; `.githooks/pre-push` refuses any push to `main`. The hook parses only git: `gh pr merge --delete-branch`, `gh pr update-branch --rebase`, `git push --mirror`/`--prune`, `git update-ref -d` and a persistent `core.hooksPath` change reach the same operations unrefused, so AGENTS.md names them as reserved too. `.githooks/pre-push` only runs where `core.hooksPath` is `.githooks`.
- **CJ's rulings on PR #67 (2026-11-11).** A pushed branch catches up by merging `origin/main` and pushing normally, never by rebase and force push. Every PR lands by merge commit, as Rigger merges (`merge_method=merge`), so the post-merge on-main check is `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch> origin/main` plus the PR's state, never `origin/<branch>`, which the forge now deletes on merge. Cards changing `docs/tech-design.md` are a new `type:tech-design` kind (architect makes, reviewer and CJ judge); other constitution, CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md changes are `type:spec`. CLAUDE.md's Rust suite is `cargo test --workspace`: the workspace's only default member is `emend`, so plain `cargo test` skipped the seven sibling crates and the P1–P6 property tests. The coordinating agent merges hand-coordinated PRs after an independent review, green CI and CJ's approval. AGENTS.md gains "Documents own their facts" (from #62). The old workflow doc's cross-model coder/reviewer preference is not carried over: Rigger's config now sets each role's provider, and hand work is expected to be rare.
- **`docs/workflow-parallel-dev.md` is removed.** Its worktree rules were already in AGENTS.md, its merge-check cleanup steps moved into AGENTS.md "Version control", its coder/reviewer loop is Rigger's maker/judge split, and its model-preference section is `rigger.config.mjs`'s providers.
- **Acceptance grep residue.** The card's acceptance grep still hits `docs/tech-design.md` (TD-21's row and status note) and five places under `docs/design/`. The first is the constitution and the second is generated, so neither is hand-edited here and both go to CJ. The pattern is case-insensitive, so ordinary design prose that says a question is CJ's to answer matches it as well as the old board column's name.

# 2026-11-10 — williacj/emend#41 comment-anchor states

- Built refreshes (32)–(34)'s anchors: selection now composes with every state (compound `.state.selected` rules recolour only the line), Working is neutral 1px dotted, Addressed — pending has its own 2px dashed amber anchor, Resolved is 1px dashed, and dark values follow `tokens.md`. Dismissed is not built; the app has no such state.
- **jsdom and `var()` in shorthands.** jsdom's `getComputedStyle` drops a `border-bottom` or `text-decoration` shorthand whose value holds a `var()`, but keeps longhands. The theme now writes longhands, so the tests read each state's width, pattern and colour token per element instead of grepping injected CSS text. Longhands also let the selected rule change only `border-bottom-color`.
- **Behaviour visible in e2e.** A thread resolved while its popover is open stays selected, so it now draws Selected + Resolved (`#E1E0F8`, 1px dashed `#6B665C`). The C4 journey asserted no selected anchor there; it now asserts that treatment in Chromium, which also checks the compound selectors win in a real cascade.

# 2026-10-06 — #2689 outer Mac app scroll

- **Test card.** Guarantee: whole-window scrolling cannot move the app shell under overflow, while long-document editor scrolling and minimum-height welcome scrolling still work. Seam: real D23 browser page, welcome surface, and editor scroll host. Killer mutations: remove viewport containment and synthetic root/body overflow makes `window.scrollY` nonzero; remove welcome's scoped scroller and its GitHub row becomes unreachable at 480px. Oracle: the UX authoring shell occupies the window and its editor owns document scrolling; the native Mac capture showed the entire shell displaced with a blank band below. Axes varied: root and body overflow, programmatic and wheel page scroll, minimum-height welcome scroll, then editor scroll. Axes fixed: one long document at desktop size after Mac-style onboarding; the original overflow source in the user's full project is not reproduced. Substitutions: none of the production participants; controlled children under root and body supply overflow pressure. Selector: `npm run test:e2e -- --grep "app page stays fixed" --workers=1` (also run in local Playwright WebKit). Before containment, the test failed with `window.scrollY === 400`; root-only clipping then exposed the welcome reachability RED; after scoped welcome scrolling and `html, body` containment, both browser engines held zero and both internal scrollers remained available. An isolated bundle-ID Mac build loaded a local copy of the affected document and kept its title, toolbar, and status visible after repeated outer scrolling, though the user's full project state has not been replayed there.

# 2026-10-06 — #2684 Windows provider probe console

- **TDD card.** Guarantee: a Windows Claude auth probe routed through a `.cmd` shim runs without an attached console while returning its output. Seam: `run_bounded` through a real temporary `.cmd` launcher into the test executable; the child reports whether `GetConsoleWindow()` is null. Killer mutation: use `group_spawn()` or set flags only on `Command` before `command_group` replaces them. Oracle: the Windows `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` contract and the Win32 console handle query, independent of Cowrite's spawn setup. Varied: `.cmd` launcher and child process boundary; fixed: a successful short probe, no actual Claude account, and the Windows test host. Substitutions: the CLI executable is a local `.cmd`; `command_group`, Win32 process creation, stdout capture, and the timeout path remain production. Selector: `cargo test --locked -p cowrite --lib agent::providers::tests::windows_cmd_probe_has_no_visible_console -- --exact` on Windows. The macOS baseline is separate and cannot execute this Windows-only cell.
- **False-negative first RED attempt.** The headless Windows runner's test process had no attached console, so its `.cmd` child also reported none before any fix; run 37512410230's Cargo test passed. The revised cell starts a dedicated test subprocess and attempts `AllocConsole` there when needed before running the production probe into the `.cmd` child. The subprocess isolates the console state from parallel Rust tests.
- **Native RED limitation.** The second Windows run 37513134945 failed in the intended test, but its log pinned the failure to `AllocConsole() == 0` in the service session, before any `.cmd` probe ran. That is setup RED, not a product RED. The parent helper now prints an explicit skip when no console can be attached. The test exercises the real `.cmd`/Win32 boundary on an interactive Windows developer machine; exact-head self-hosted CI can compile and execute the safe skip but cannot prove visible-window absence. The installed app report is the current interactive pre-fix observation. The fix uses `command_group`'s Windows builder flag, as its own `with_flags` example documents; setting `Command::creation_flags` before `group_spawn` would be overwritten by `command_group`'s spawn implementation.
- **Independent review.** A separate reviewer found the builder-level `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` change sound and no blocking production issue. The same review identified `AgentSession::spawn_inner` as a separate provider-turn path still using plain `group_spawn`; #2686 records that later signed-in-run console risk. It does not explain this signed-out startup/focus loop. The reviewer agreed the service-runner console test is partial evidence and that interactive Finn-PC validation remains necessary.
- **Local gates.** The pre-change full Rust baseline passed with loopback permission; its sandboxed attempt failed existing socket-binding tests. On the fix branch, the full Rust workspace suite, full-target Clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `git diff --check`, and the matrix definition check passed. A browser run with symlinked dependencies failed unrelated font/layout cases because Vite rejected fonts outside the worktree. After `npm ci --offline`, 139 browser cases passed, and eight late cases failed solely because their test fixture hard-codes the worktree `src-tauri/target/debug/test-driver` path while Cargo had built in the shared target. Supplying that target path and rerunning exactly those eight J7/#2540 cases passed 8/8. The source-stamped Windows PR gate and interactive installed-app validation remain separate.

# 2026-10-04 — #2679 turn-15 chooser visual parity

- **Independent-review held-state evidence.** Test card: guarantee—D23 queued-held comments show the held scope ending in an em dash, a 14px unchecked checkbox with a disabled `Address 0 comments`, then an enabled `Address 2 comments` after explicit opt-in, in wide light and narrow dark frames. Seam: production D23 `address_all_with_ai` targeting two real threads, Vite chooser, source-stamped manifest and PNGs. Killer mutation: omit the em dash, leave held targets enabled by default, ignore the opt-in, or render a checked/disabled style as plain controls. Oracle: refresh (26) `specs/comments.md` § Address all chooser; varied width, theme, opt-in; fixed two open queued-held comments; no product participant substituted. Selector: `node --test --test-name-pattern='turn 15 held chooser evidence' scripts/capture-design-alignment.test.mjs`. RED lacked the four held frames. The first D23 fixture attempt reused the first returned thread ID because `create_comment` returns the whole list; selecting the newly minted ID corrected the fixture. GREEN captures the two transitions at both widths and themes.
- **Test card, 900px anchor.** Guarantee: the moved chooser opens 4px below the visible ⋯ with right edges aligned. Seam: mounted `BatchAddressTrigger` with toolbar overflow context and measured invoker. Killer mutation: keep measuring its hidden menu-row trigger. Oracle: refresh (26) `specs/comments.md` § Address all chooser, `15b`. Vary visible vs hidden invoker; fix a 220px chooser and 900px viewport. Substitute only geometry; production placement and React remain mounted. Selector: `npx vitest run src/comments/BatchAddressTrigger.test.tsx -t 'anchors a moved'`. RED was top 4px from the hidden trigger; GREEN is top 50px from the visible invoker.
- **Test card, browser evidence.** Guarantee: every Win32/MacIntel light/dark 1280/900 capture has a single toolbar row, bundled font, 4px right-aligned chooser, and at 900px a closed overflow menu with its visible ⋯ painted open. Seam: the production Vite+D23 visual capture and committed manifest. Killer mutation: anchor to the hidden menu row or lose an entire platform/theme/width frame. Oracle: refresh (26) `15a`/`15b` and `specs/comments.md`. Vary platform style, theme and width; fix two real open comments and a collapsed Timeline. No production participant is replaced. Selector: `node --test --test-name-pattern='turn 15 chooser evidence' scripts/capture-design-alignment.test.mjs`. RED was a missing manifest; GREEN contains eight captures. Browser platform styling does not establish native Windows chrome.
- The standalone Timeline host now floats its chooser so its header stays one row. The chooser follows turn 15's panel, select, checkbox, action and focus palette in both themes; held scope copy ends with an em dash. The toolbar trigger uses the specified Windows border instead of the undefined `--timeline-facet-border` in that host. A CSS rule test went RED on the absent inline group hairline, then GREEN with the 10px inset and no hairline in overflow. The 900px overflow button retains its open fill while the chooser is up. Generated `docs/design` was not hand-edited; refresh (26)'s M11 note predates CJ's J4/#2676 ruling and #2678.

- [2026-10-04] **#2345 refused-close focus test limit (CJ decision).** The finalization path restores focus from an effect after the editor pane's `inert` freeze lifts. Its vitest assertions prove the restore request and destination, but jsdom does not enforce `inert`; D23 cannot reach the native refused-close route because `isTauri()` is false there. No current automated test can distinguish the effect from an early microtask in a runtime that enforces `inert`. CJ chose to record this known gap in the test matrix and at the effect, satisfying #2345's documentation disposition; a future native runtime test should target the ordering if that route becomes testable.

- [2026-10-04] **#2258 review follow-up test card — submitting project owns delayed admissions.** Guarantee: a single-thread Address started in project A cannot raise a queued or failed run card in project B if its admission settles after the project switch. Seam: mounted App, real popover and project-open route, deferred backend admission. Killer mutation: App appends a callback result to its unkeyed incoming-card list without checking the submitting root. Oracle: the run was submitted against A, while B's Timeline belongs to B (FR-CMT-2/TD-15). Varied: A then B before queued success, then rejected admission; fixed: one anchored thread and identical `notes/shared.md` path per project to isolate root identity. Substitutions: only IPC responses and the deferred admission; production App/editor/Timeline participants remain mounted. Selector: `npx vitest run src/App.test.tsx -t 'late Address admission from project A'`. The queued case failed RED with A's cancellable card in B, then passed when both single-thread producers carried their root through App. The rejected case failed RED with A's failure heading in B when the guard was removed, then passed with the same root guard restored. - [2026-10-04] **#2333 permanent queue/emitter race pin (FR-X-3, #2329 follow-up).** Test card: guarantee — an assertion on a terminal activity push must wait on the emitter, since queue finalization precedes that push; seam — real `RunQueue` plus `fake-agent` and a test-only `RecordingEmitter`; killer mutation — replace any of the four emitter waits with a queue wait and one emitter snapshot; oracle — `ActivityEmitter` runs only after the queue lock drops; varied — answer, empty delivery, landed delivery, and full activity feed; fixed — one run with no queued successor; substitution — the emitter only. No product code or assertion-path sleep. `RunObserver` was checked: its callbacks run before finalization and provide no second post-terminal status transport, so moving this pin there would not exercise this gap.

- [2026-10-02] **#2623/P5-4a stalled model-download Cancel, backend slice.** CJ approved an interruptible async reqwest producer feeding the existing blocking integrity/resume downloader through a capacity-two channel. A per-attempt token races both response headers and body chunks, wakes the blocking reader, and aborts/joins the producer before the per-model lock is released. An accepted Cancel sets `Cancelling` before network cancellation finishes; a typed `Cancelled` follows only after the retained partial is synced. A completion claim prevents a late accepted Cancel from relabeling a verified publish. Download notices now carry a monotonic revision assigned with the owner phase; a held Progress callback can arrive after Cancelling but has an older revision, and status carries the same revision. D23 asserts the serialized `{revision, phase}` envelope; callbacks may arrive out of revision order. TDD cells went RED→GREEN for the held Settings status, held open, held body/resume, async connect/body await, completion arbitration, delayed event delivery, and D23 event route. The held-network tests use channels and five-second condition waits without releasing the stall; they do not claim a hard bound for pathological local fsync, digest hash, or registry-lock latency. Gate A: full Rust workspace passed with loopback/watcher permission (the sandboxed baseline had the known 51 permission failures); frontend passed 133 files/2,212 tests. Final gates: full Rust workspace, no-default-feature voice, Clippy, frontend 134 files/2,226 tests, frontend build, Playwright 144 passed/2 skipped, fmt, and matrix 3,879 definitions passed. Clippy needed `GGML_CCACHE=OFF` after the native cache launcher stalled; Playwright needed a local ignored `node_modules` copy so Vite served bundled fonts. #2624 must consume notice revisions and implement Settings/shared mic presentation before the Cancel promise is user-facing complete.

- [2026-10-02] **#2616 independent review correction: Start acceptance is distinct from current status.** The original Start command emitted Recording for generation A under the owner lock, then read status after releasing it. Cancel A and Start B could complete during that read, so A's response could expose B's generation and launch a second worker against B. A channel-held post-accept status read now tests that exact order without sleeps: A's flattened command receipt retains `accepted_generation=A`, its current status truthfully reports B, and an A update cannot poll B. The test first failed to compile on the absent receipt/seam, then passed with the immutable receipt. A temporary mutation selecting A's accepted generation from the later status made the held-order test RED (`2 != 1`), and restoration returned GREEN. The Tauri command now launches its worker from the accepted field. The frontend mirror exposes the same separate field to #2549/#2550 leases. No transcript insertion was added. Corrected-head gates: 909 Rust library tests passed (3 ignored) plus workspace integrations with loopback/watcher permissions, linked-inference focused selector, no-default Cargo check, Clippy all targets, frontend 133 files/2,212 tests, production build, matrix 3,846/3,846 definitions, and browser 144 passed/2 skipped.

- [2026-10-02] **#2616 correlated live dictation stream (bounded split from #2549).** Gate A on reviewed #2548 `cd9344028`: frontend 133 files/2,212 tests, Rust workspace 902 library tests passed (3 ignored) and integrations green with local loopback/watcher permissions, browser 144 passed/2 skipped. The Start-status/event generation contract failed compilation on missing fields, then passed; a live snapshot test failed compilation on absent `update_dictation`, then emitted a generation-tagged Partial before Stop; inference and capture-snapshot failures each failed on a stuck owner, then retired capture and emitted correlated typed Failed. A paused Tokio-clock worker test failed compilation on the missing timer loop, then passed at 749/750 ms and after cancel/restart, with no wall-clock sleep. A held live inference test proved Cancel plus a newer Start suppresses the old Partial and ends its worker; temporarily returning `Ok(true)` for a cancelled update made its worker-exit assertion RED, and restoration returned GREEN. A model-loss test covers typed `ModelMissing`/`ModelCorrupt` after Start, paired with #2544's verified-byte Whisper preflight; collapsing Missing to generic inference failure made the test RED, and restoration returned GREEN. The D23 cursor event log test passes the production typed Partial payload and generation through to the frontend channel. The normal Tauri Start command launches one sequential 750 ms capture-snapshot worker per accepted generation; Stop/Cancel/restart fence it at the Rust owner. The 750 ms interval bounds only the wait until the next snapshot and avoids overlapping full-window inference; SPIKE-5's p95 <16 ms target measures CM6 ghost dispatch, not microphone/inference/IPC latency, so this card claims no end-to-end partial-latency figure. `VoiceStatus.dictation_generation` and every `voice://dictation` phase event now carry the accepted generation; the typed frontend event helper is ready for #2549 editor and #2550 composer leases. No transcript insertion occurs in this card. Post-change gates: frontend 133 files/2,212 tests, Rust workspace and integrations green before the final model-loss test, linked-inference paused-worker selector green, no-default Cargo check, Clippy all targets, production web build, and matrix definition check green. A browser run under concurrent Rust load had one unrelated J2 fixture-cleanup timeout; its focused rerun passed in 756 ms, and the uncontended full browser suite then passed 144/144 with 2 skipped. Final exact-head Rust and matrix counts are recorded in handoff.

RED found no provision step; GREEN provisions the NuGet `libclang.runtime.win-x64` 22.1.8 package (Microsoft windows-rs precedent), verifies SHA-256 `676b4aa346bc793af2fa05d80a9933825202ba65f71230417d5f0bb191dc3662`, publishes its native DLL directory, and clears the invalid override. The downloaded 30 MB package contains `runtimes/win-x64/native/libclang.dll`; README and runner setup now require Windows target binding generation for manual builds.

- [2026-10-02] **#2547 independent-review corrections, four Important findings.** (1) A mounted Settings section offered a duplicate Download and hid Cancel on reopen because `downloading` required its local `busy` flag even when Rust reported `Progress`; a remount cell went RED on absent Cancel, then GREEN when backend phase alone chose the action. - [2026-10-02] **#2545/P5-2a local model registry.** The Rust `voice::model_registry` seam stores model ID, canonical path, trusted expected byte length and BLAKE3 digest under app-local data; a fixed companion OS lock serializes cross-window manifest updates and atomic JSON replacement publishes complete metadata. `admit` validates the candidate before changing the registry; `status` reads the durable record and hashes the current file before returning Ready, distinguishing Absent, Missing, length/digest Corrupt, and invalid registry metadata (an error, never a false Ready). A new instance reads back a valid admission; a rejected candidate leaves a prior Ready record; valid repair re-admits. The first relaunch cell was RED on the intentionally missing API and GREEN after implementation; focused filesystem cells cover missing, changed length, same-length digest corruption, rejected candidate preservation, and corrupt-file recovery without mocked I/O. Gate A passed on the unchanged worktree with OS loopback/watch access; the sandboxed attempt had 51 unrelated permission failures.

- [2026-10-01] **#2537 independent-review corrections.** Four review risks received discriminating RED→GREEN cells. - [2026-10-01] **#2595 B4b overflow focus race.** Test card: guarantee—the focused Bulk action keeps keyboard focus when it returns from `⋯` to the inline toolbar, including repeated 330→500→660px resize interactions; seam—real Chromium toolbar, ResizeObserver, and D23 transport; killer mutation—clear the return-focus request before its inline slot registers; oracle—NI-editor-toolbar-b4b's recorded B4b focus recovery; varied—20 consecutive resize cycles with the same focused action; fixed—Windows styling, one tracked insertion and document; substitutions—backend transport only; selector—`npm run test:e2e -- --grep 'B4b restores keyboard focus when overflow changes'`. The unchanged product failed RED in two of the first five repeated cells (cycles 9 and 16); the original clean-main full suite had failed the same assertion once. At failure, keyboard focus was on the selected Rich text fallback. Browser logging showed the parent layout effect consumed `review-bulk`'s focus request while that slot still lived in the portal and its inline ref was absent. The handoff now waits for the inline ref to register before focusing Bulk; a removed action still uses the selected-view fallback. The same 20-cycle cell passed 20/20 repetitions after the fix. It adds about 1.5 seconds to the one full-suite cell.

The corrected cell checks the two real `address` events in the TD-4 sidecar (the second has the submitted guidance and a distinct run id), then waits for that second run's folded `failed` conclusion and checks the honest failed UI. - [2026-10-01] **#2581 D23 boundary diagnosis and correction.** The original focused browser journey failed locally after resize: one failure was 0.094px past the scroll-host edge, while another left the final nonempty line 26.47px below it. At the latter failure, CodeMirror's selection head equaled the full 242,436-character document length and its end caret measured at y=893.47–914.47 against a host ending at y=864; this was a real offscreen caret. Independent review required a direct end-caret assertion in addition to the last-content-line assertion; that exact new assertion failed RED on the original production editor at repeat 9/20. A one- or two-frame host scroll also failed because CodeMirror subsequently changed measured height and re-anchored the viewport. The corrected `DocumentSurface` holds the end-navigation intent while that view remains focused and selected at the document end, checks actual caret geometry after scroll/height/viewport updates, and scrolls its actual host only when the caret falls below it. User wheel, pointer, selection, blur, and view destruction cancel the intent. The corrected journey passed 40 consecutive real Chromium repetitions with the caret assertion; the full browser and current-head Windows gates follow. The geometry checks permit only 1px of fractional CSS edge rounding.

- [2026-10-01] **J10 manual Resolve integration guard (FR-CMT-21(a/b)).** The existing header Resolve could close a thread with undecided linked changes while J10's new menu correctly refused it; both path-backed and pathless direct commands could also bypass the UI. Manual Resolve now folds, checks, and appends under the comment write transaction, returning `ClosureRefused` without an event when any linked change is undecided. The fold also refuses to close over undecided work when P5 timestamp ordering replays a legal Resolve after a later delivery. A run in flight does not block Resolve. A later delivery with a genuinely new linked change reopens the thread for review, while an empty/duplicate delivery or defense-only reply leaves manual closure in place, as the J4 exit table requires. Focused command and fold regressions cover route shapes, stale direct calls, no append, and delivery order. Full FR-CMT-21 remains pending until J11 Dismiss implements the same closure rule.

- [2026-10-01] **J10 pathless correction test card (FR-CMT-8/9/12, J12).** Guarantee: an already-minted pathless document id can revise and withdraw its own comment and reply through the same fold/check/append lane as file-backed correction, using current supplied text for the returned anchor and writing no project document file. A foreign supplied author and a root with a live child are refused without an append. Seam: `revise_comment_by_id`/`withdraw_comment_by_id` over a real `SidecarStore` and minted ID. Killer mutation: route `_by_id` through the unchecked generic append helper, or open a synthetic path instead of the existing id. Oracle: D24/J10 child-first and J12's pathless comment accumulation. Axes varied: root/reply, owner/foreign author, live child/no live child, and live content revision; fixed: one project and one process, no authenticated actor identity or agent Address. The missing `_by_id` functions produced the intended compile RED; the shared guarded helper and pathless wrappers passed GREEN. Full unsandboxed Rust workspace suite passed with 834 library tests, 3 ignored, and all integration targets green. These caller-supplied-author functions remain unregistered in Tauri pending a backend-owned actor wrapper.

A private root with only withdrawn own replies disappears, a historical other-author reply keeps context, and a late live own reply remains visible. Killer mutations: infer publication from local create/reply, drop `Publish` during the fold, or treat any historical reply as another actor's. Oracle: D20 and J10 Phase B/C, including the late-offline exception. Axes varied: explicit publication, historical reply author, late live reply, causal arrival before root, and malformed reply target; fixed: one document, no real review sync. Substitutions: none. The agent `reply_to_comment` tool now re-folds and checks its target while holding `comment_transaction` through the append, returning `WithdrawnComment`/JSON-RPC invalid params for an already withdrawn root or reply and persisting no child. - [2026-10-01] **J10 independent-review agent-read and race-proof corrections.** Review found that `comments_view` filtered a withdrawn root before considering its late live child. A focused test failed RED because the agent saw only the unrelated live root; GREEN now includes the late reply beneath a bodyless, anchorless, severityless parent with explicit `withdrawn: true`, while keeping withdrawn reply text absent. A test-only probe now combines this thread's transaction depth with `COMMENT_WRITE_LOCK.try_lock() == WouldBlock` at correction, single Address, and batch Address validation points; bypassing the mutex makes the relevant test fail deterministically, while real JSONL outcome assertions remain. A late live grandchild under a withdrawn intermediate reply also keeps root Delete refused until that grandchild is withdrawn. All three corrections stay within the unregistered internal path. - [2026-10-01] **J10 independent-review queue-to-event hold.** The local write lane begins only when `address_with_ai` or `address_all_with_ai` folds and appends an Address event. The existing UI calls `startRun` first, then writes that event; a run can therefore already be accepted or queued with captured comment text while the fold still reports no `pending_run`. A correction admitted in that interval would violate FR-CMT-10's "from the moment queued" rule. The correction commands remain Tauri-unregistered, so this is a release gate rather than a newly exposed regression. A future command-level admission must atomically reserve the comment target with queue acceptance (or establish an equivalent ordering) before correction can be exposed. The test matrix and command docs now scope this card's concurrency claim to the persisted Address event; PR #2505 stays draft and unmerged.
- [2026-10-01] **J10 child-first internal correction result.** The 2026-09-30 cascade-hide test and its private-root journal card are superseded by the two current cards below. Folded replies now retain direct ancestry for the command gate; a root or nested reply refuses withdrawal over every live descendant, including other-author and resolved-thread replies, and an admitted parent withdrawal appends no child suppression. A late same-author or other-author reply makes an otherwise hidden private withdrawn root visible as a bodyless placeholder. `SidecarStore` serializes ordinary comment appends and exposes one in-process transaction spanning the correction or persisted Address eligibility fold and append; single and batch Address take that lane. The correction commands remain unregistered in Tauri, and this branch must remain draft/unmerged until the queue-to-event hold above and card, popover, timeline and count/highlight projection can render withdrawal honestly. A published root with no reply cannot yet leave the required placeholder because the root event/fold has no D20 publication bit; actor identity remains caller-supplied and pathless correction is absent. These are explicit next-card dependencies, not satisfied by this internal pass.

The unsandboxed full Rust suite passed (823/823 library tests, 3 ignored, all integration suites); `npm test -- --run` passed 2,081/2,081 and matrix definitions passed 3,603/3,603. The first browser run used a symlinked dependency tree outside Vite's font allow list and failed three typography cases for that environment reason; an isolated `npm ci --offline --ignore-scripts` fixed the dependency location, and the full browser suite passed 112 tests with 2 Windows-only skips. Formatting and diff checks passed. These gates exercise the internal path after Address persistence; Tauri registration and user-facing withdrawal remain held for the queue-to-event, UI and D20 dependencies above.

- [2026-09-30] **D24 / J10 child-first withdrawal ruling.** CJ replaced the private-root cascade-hide behavior: a parent comment or nested reply cannot be withdrawn until every descendant reply is withdrawn by its own author. Another actor can therefore block that parent indefinitely in v1; owner moderation awaits a permissions model. The command checks the current fold before appending, while a later-converging offline reply remains visible beneath a withdrawn placeholder so the append-only sync contract loses nothing. A withdrawn reply stops blocking its parent while its existing nested provenance line may remain visible when the thread is shown; the child-first ruling sets no new placeholder policy for that line after parent withdrawal. The generated `docs/design/` mocks still need the child-present inert Delete state and revised confirmations; the handoff is `docs/j10-child-first-visual-refresh-brief.md`.

- [2026-09-30] **D27 `.Txt` save-oracle review correction.** Independent review found the initial no-edit byte assertion could pass before any save completed. The focused follow-up exposed a stronger gap: after the CRLF opt-out button was clicked, the editor lacked keyboard focus, so the first Ctrl/Cmd+S did not issue a document write. The browser cell now focuses CodeMirror, awaits the real `save_document` HTTP response, checks that it carried the original source and `normalizeLineEndings: false`, waits for the Windows-style visible `Saved · …` Document status, and only then compares disk bytes. The focused D23 selector passes. The rest of the `.Txt` card remains test-only.

- [2026-09-30] **J10 correction slice integration hold (independent review).** This branch is merge-blocked. The new `resolved_threads` projection can emit a withdrawn root with a later other-actor reply, but the current `CommentThreadCard`, comment popover, and timeline do not read `withdrawn_at`; they would display a blank ordinary thread with ordinary actions, count, and anchor treatment instead of FR-CMT-9's residue placeholder. Keep the branch out of production until the placeholder/card projection and related count/highlight behavior are implemented and reviewed. Separately, the correction command's fold/check/append and Address targeting must share a serialized writer before Tauri registration, so FR-CMT-10's fail-closed concurrency claim remains blocked. J10 Phase C explicitly preserves every reply beneath a revealed residue placeholder, including private replies by the root author; their earlier cascade concealment ends when another actor replies. These are integration prerequisites, not known-passing acceptance cells.

- [2026-09-30] **J10 internal file-backed correction slice result.** `Revise`/`Withdraw` are published append-only event types; the fold keeps revision/withdrawal times, clears withdrawn bodies, ignores corrections from a different recorded author, and never lets a later revision resurrect withdrawal. File-backed correction commands check direct-target ownership, terminal state, targeting by a queued/not-started address run, empty revision and undecided-change withdrawal, then append to the same author's JSONL. The current working projection hides an unpublished withdrawn root with only its own replies and restores a bodyless placeholder with every reply when another actor later replies; TD-1 agent state filters withdrawn text; single/batch address paths exclude withdrawn roots. The Tauri invoke list intentionally does **not** register correction commands: the service has no serialized fold/check/append boundary shared with address targeting, so the checks can race a new target and cannot yet claim FR-CMT-10 fail-closed concurrency. The IPC `author` argument is caller supplied, so comparing it with target ownership is not authenticated actor identity. D20 published-root state, pathless commands, and all J10 UI/gallery behavior remain future cards. Focused red/green selectors passed; full unsandboxed `cargo test --quiet` passed (819 library tests: 816 pass, 3 ignored, plus all integration suites), `npm test -- --run` passed (2,060/2,060), TypeScript passed and the matrix definition check passed (3,566 names). The sandboxed baseline failed 51 preexisting loopback/native-watch tests with `Operation not permitted`; unsandboxed full suite cleared that environment limit.

Seam: `store::comments::fold` over explicit cross-author event histories. Killer mutations: rewriting the create body, letting a revise after withdrawal restore visibility, or losing a correction whose parent arrives later. Axes varied: root/reply, revision/withdrawal, causal arrival order, and revision time. Fixed: one document, valid event envelope, no run or linked change. Substitutions: none. - [2026-09-07] **#2429 review correction: a fallback contract needs transition identity, not only the right destinations.** Three independently reproduced sequences crossed otherwise-green endpoints. Explicit cue activation was replayed when the same FIFO head received a later revision, queued growth erased the fact that the surviving head's subtree owned focus before that head cleared, and a no-visible-growth permission revision left a scroll snapshot for an unrelated later append to consume. The fixes make each lifetime explicit: consume a navigation nonce after its heading actually receives focus; retain subtree focus ownership while that request id survives; and settle each permission scroll snapshot after the row-measurement commit, clearing it when no visible growth occurred. CJ's ratified clear-focus supplement closes the fourth review finding without inventing UXR-Q13: the expanded host now keeps one real non-actionable level-2 `Timeline` heading mounted. A clearing focused attention action prefers the eventual owner's connected, focusable collapse toggle, but production currently has none, so it focuses that heading with `preventScroll` and an unconditional 2 px accent outline. A clear when the expanded action did not own focus and a new request while the heading is focused remain passive; ordinary DOM Tab order and project-replacement recovery are unchanged.

- [2026-09-07] **#2429: permission attention is navigation only when the author asks for it.** The D23 broker already retained a run-bound request while a facet filtered out its only decision surface; the Timeline renderer simply had no unfiltered chrome derived from that state. The expanded generated Timeline label now carries one nonnumeric Pause badge and one polite once-per-FIFO-head announcement, while arrival leaves the facet, scroll owner, editor/review state, and DOM focus alone. Permission-FIFO growth also snapshots the virtual list's scroll position before row measurement so a reader at the tail is not moved merely because an existing run changed; the ordinary new-card tail-follow path remains unchanged. Activating the cue selects Chat, targets the exact run in the virtual stream, expands it, and focuses a labelled request heading; decisions and Stop remain on that one card. Permission-subtree removal moves focus to the next head or the run status, retaining an immediately completed card expanded when necessary. Project replacement after that explicit navigation returns focus to its connected origin, or to the new project's programmatically focusable Timeline host when the origin is gone. The current app still has no collapsed Timeline/comments-column owner, so a reusable collapsed-host seam pins stable-button clearing and the Windows pending overflow exception without answering UXR-Q13's open empty-state, window-scope, or composer questions. The real regression uses the production frontend, D23 backend, MCP `permission_request`, and schema-validated deterministic provider under Changes; it fails against the original code because no cue exists while the backend request remains pending. OS rendering remains separate. Implementation cleanup was `inline simplify (fan-out unavailable)`: removed a redundant element ref, kept one permission-owner map, corrected the header action wrapper to valid block markup, and kept the scroll exception keyed only to permission-FIFO identity changes.

- [2026-09-07] **#2399 native Windows validation: callback syntax is not native filesystem spelling.** The first Windows gate exposed two positive script fixtures that interpolated native backslashes into the deliberately literal Bash grammar. Fixture callback paths now use forward slashes, as the existing browser provider does; filesystem reads and the production matcher remain unchanged. Positive eligibility checks precede invalid-command and stale-content assertions so a malformed fixture cannot masquerade as a denied grant. The same run caught a redundant String conversion, removed without a lint suppression.

## 2026-09-07 — #2399: preserve stale-script recovery guidance

Review found that a refused script grant correctly left the callback pending but discarded the backend’s actionable failure on the run card. The existing `errorDetail` reader now carries that message into the existing inline status; Deny remains available. A typed-error component regression fails when only generic retry text is rendered. Unreadable errors retain the existing generic fallback. No permission grant or cancellation behavior changes.

- [2026-09-07] **#2397: a parser-applied class is not automatically governed by a decoration builder's source-view gate.** The structural builder already returned no ranges in Markdown view, but `syntaxHighlighting(headingHighlightStyle)` independently attached heading typography, leaving H1–H6 bold and H1–H5 enlarged. CJ clarified TD-2's “decorations off” contract directly: source headings use body size, weight, and line height while their markers remain visible. The smallest fix keeps the sanctioned CM6 tag-highlighting seam and gives its heading tags stable classes; the editor's live source-view attribute gates the rich typography rules. A real Chromium regression compares every text-bearing H1–H6 span with a body line by computed metrics, so it follows the active document font instead of pinning today’s pixel values, and saves the unchanged Markdown bytes.

- [2026-09-07] **#2399: CJ ratified changed arguments under a run-local script grant.** To “May same-run script approval cover changed arguments when the verified project path and script contents are unchanged?”, CJ answered “Yes.” D14 and J7 record that ruling. The backend now verifies a canonical project-contained absolute script path and a bounded content digest, binds the invocation's interpreter and execution/security controls, and rechecks before a waiting decision and later reuse. A relative path has no inferred cwd; compound, dynamic, outside-project and unresolvable forms have no script scope. Real MCP tests start at ProjectMcpServer, approve the script, reuse changed arguments, then require a new decision after content/control/run changes. The browser journey uses the real callback and existing script menu item. The native callback envelope and D15 ephemerality remain unchanged. Callback-time identity checking does not freeze filesystem contents until external execution. The absent timeline attention host is separately tracked in #2429.

- [2026-09-07] **#2399: a permission callback must bind to the spawning run, not whichever run is current when it arrives.** Claude 2.1.263 actually calls the configured MCP permission tool with `tool_name`, object `input`, and optional `tool_use_id`; that provider wire shape needs an explicit adapter onto Cowrite's unchanged TD-1 `action/detail/why` service contract. A bounded real-Claude probe initialized and discovered the server, requested a synthetic Python write, received denial guidance, and returned `PERMISSION_DENIED_WITNESS` without creating the file. The project bearer survives sequential runs, so the backend additionally injects `X-Cowrite-Run`; absent or stale run identity is denied before a prompt is created. The broker owns pending input, FIFO decisions and run-local grants; Stop revokes admission before killing the child and terminal cleanup releases waiters as deny. Permission never authorizes a protected-document delivery outside TD-1/D21.

- [2026-09-07] **#2399: a safe file outcome is not evidence that the provider accepted a denial.** The first production-backend smoke completed without creating the synthetic file, but its final reply reported an invalid permission result. The successful standalone probe differed in one important way: text-only JSON instead of duplicate `structuredContent`. A discriminating TCP regression and a second actual-Claude smoke confirmed that callback-specific text-only envelope: denial returned `PERMISSION_DENIED_WITNESS`, Allow once created its separate synthetic witness, and both runs cleared controls. Keep the generic MCP transport test, but use an actual provider witness to verify permission-result semantics. Exact-command grants exclude display `description` while retaining command text and execution/security fields.

- [2026-09-06] **#1750: cross-process focus is an owner acknowledgement, not a requester inference.** The requester treats `owner.json` only as an address after it positively proves a live Cowrite process and matching executable/bundle identity; the OS lock remains ownership authority. Windows grants the validated PID before a durable request is armed. macOS first writes and syncs the exact request in the already-locked staging lane, calls `NSRunningApplication.activate(options: [])`, then atomically arms the request, preventing the owner watcher from outrunning activation. The lock-owning `OpenProject` owns the answer listener and drops it before releasing its claim. The owner unminimizes (and shows on macOS when needed), focuses, verifies state, and only then writes `Focused`; a refused grant or a failed acknowledgement stays an ordinary bounded failure, while a window already raised before a failed publication is intentionally not fictionalized as rolled back. Windows real PID/image validation runs in the suite; macOS source/package and visual focus evidence remains a separate-host merge prerequisite.

- [2026-09-06] **#2119 review loopback: adoption changes capability, not the mounted status owner.** A Save As-adopted pathless tab deliberately remains on `UntitledDocument` to preserve its TD-24 CM6 surface, so the ordinary `EditorShell` comment-mode wire cannot cover it. `TabbedEditor` now forwards its existing active-tab Timeline mode into that retained branch; `UntitledDocument` derives open/resolved counts from the comment projection it already owns and admits the binding comment sequence only once `adoptedPath` exists. The regression drives real adoption, proves pathless-before-adoption still exposes no comment capability, then proves `Markdown · words · open · resolved` replaces the tracked-change fact without replacing the `EditorView`.

- [2026-09-06] **#2119: a document status bar is a projection of existing owners, including across pathless adoption.** #2253 already supplied the overflow-aware shell, #2120 supplied Display, #2109 supplied save/recovery state, and #1803 supplied TD-24's shared path-free CM6 surface. The remaining status content therefore reads tracked changes directly from that surface's mounted state rather than adding another parser, timer, or `trackedChangeCount` owner. Review exposed that the same ownership rule includes performance: materializing and regex-scanning the whole CM6 document in React render made every edit O(document), so word starts now live in one CM6 `StateField`. Its initial scan is exact and subsequent transactions subtract/add merged changed ranges plus two code points of boundary context; deterministic randomized Unicode and multi-range equivalence cells compare every projection with the canonical full count, while a 5 MB one-code-unit edit proves only a constant inspection window. Canonical tracked-change parse requests keep #1803's exact request/view/content fences and retain the last established count while pending or rejected; importantly, a still-current response releases the pending latch before rejecting content staleness, while a superseded request can never clear a newer request's latch. Open-comment counts fold the active document owner's supplied threads and stay absent—not zero—until a pathless document is adopted. The Timeline remains the sole facet owner and projects only whether its Comments facet is active; that mode selects the binding `Markdown · words · open · resolved` sequence, suppresses tracked changes, and follows live resolve/reopen without remounting the active editor. The same `EditorView`, undo history, selection, focus, Display control, and status row cross adoption; only the capability facts change. Provider detection is likewise App-owned because invoking the hook per tab would multiply bounded CLI subprocesses on mount and every focus refresh. A rejected transport is reported as unavailable rather than invented as `not installed`, and a generation fence prevents an older overlapping focus probe from replacing the newest result. The reusable rule is literal: status is projection, never a second state machine for the fact it labels.

- [2026-09-05] **SPIKE-6/#1745: cross-process project routing is viable on both measured hosts, but activation is a sequence, not a focus call.** Real Windows 11 25H2 and macOS 26.6.2 runs cleared TD-20's launch and `notify` assumptions: Windows detached exec left 30/30 children alive after their launchers exited (13.419 ms max ready), macOS `open -n -a` produced 10/10 distinct live bundle processes and its `setsid` fallback passed 20/20, and 2,500 native outcome notifications per OS stayed below 28.254 ms against the 3-second raise budget. The negative controls supplied the implementation rule: `set_focus()` cannot raise a minimized tao window on either OS; Windows grant + restore + focus succeeded, while macOS yield + restore + focus failed 10/10 and explicit `NSRunningApplication.activate` + restore/show + focus succeeded 10/10 from a background grant process. P2a-11f should use the explicit macOS activation route, preserve unminimize/show-before-focus, verify observed focus before acknowledging, and keep the bounded failure path. TD-20 option C and a product reconsideration are not required on these hosts; no recorded-decision delta is proposed. Full findings: [`docs/spikes/spike6-second-instance-activation.md`](spikes/spike6-second-instance-activation.md).

- [2026-09-03] **#2268: three review rounds each fixed a token comparison at the position it was shown and left the identical defect one position over. What finally held was normalizing the input, not patching another comparison.** Each individual fix was correct: round 1 inverted the subcommand check from a denylist to an allowlist but left `addressesSharedStack` — a denylist — nested inside it; round 2 stripped redirections at the OPERAND position (`stash apply > /dev/null`) and left them at the INVOCATION position (`git >/dev/null stash pop`, where the leading-flag walk exits on the first non-`-` token and discards the command), case-folded the `.exe` suffix but not `git` itself (Windows resolves PATH case-insensitively, so `GIT stash pop` ran), and matched `refs/stash` but not `refs/STASH` (loose refs are files, so a case-insensitive filesystem resolves both to the same oid). **The generalizable rule: when a guard compares tokens, the defect is rarely the comparison you were shown — it is a CLASS of input, and the class reaches every comparison.** The remedy that ends the sequence removes the class before any comparison sees it: one `SHELL_NOISE` normalization strips redirections and line continuations from the tail once, so the global-option walk, the subcommand check and the operand walk all become correct together instead of each learning about redirections separately. **The complementary discipline, to be done BEFORE pushing a guard, is to enumerate every position where a token is compared and settle each by execution rather than intuition.** Doing that here produced a table now recorded in the hook header, and it is not uniform: the command word is case-INSENSITIVE (`GIT rev-parse` executes), refs are case-INSENSITIVE (`git rev-parse refs/STASH` returns the stash oid), the subcommand is case-SENSITIVE (`git STASH list` → "'STASH' is not a git command", so folding it would only add false blocks), and option names must NOT be folded because `-c` and `-C` are different git flags. Guessing uniformly would have been wrong in both directions at once.

- [2026-09-03] **#2268: a parse failure and a genuine non-match look identical at the call site (`continue`) unless the guard tells them apart, and only one of them is safe to let pass.** Round 4 found `&>`, `>|`, and process substitution as three more `SHELL_NOISE`-shaped operators (closed in the one regex, alongside round 2's family, rather than as three patches) and a missing `--attr-source` in the value-taking global-option set — both ordinary instances of the round-1/round-2 pattern. The fourth finding was structural rather than another instance: a partial strip of `&>` left the residue `" & stash pop"`, and `tokenizeArgs`'s `isSep` breaks on the leftover `&` before a single token is read, so `findStashInvocations` returned `[]` — indistinguishable, at `if (tokens[i] !== "stash") continue`, from `git commit`, a command that is genuinely not about stash and genuinely safe to pass. **The generalizable rule: an allowlist guard has (at least) three outcomes, not two — matched-safe, matched-unsafe, and *could not be classified* — and collapsing the third into "safe" is a silent fail-open no comparison-level fix can catch, because by construction there is no token left to compare.** The fix is not a better `&>` pattern (round-4 item 1 already supplies one); it is refusing to conflate "no comparison" with "a passing comparison" — `findStashInvocations` now reports an unparsed tail as its own explicit outcome (told apart from a genuinely bare `git`, which has nothing to parse at all, by whether the raw pre-strip tail carried any content), and `decide()` refuses it with a dedicated message. This closes the class the first three items are instances of: the next shell spelling this file has not enumerated now costs a bypass env var, like every other unrecognised form here, instead of a silent pass. **CORRECTION (2026-09-04, round 5): this "closes the class" claim was false, and so was the identical claim in the 2026-09-03 entry above about `SHELL_NOISE` making "the global-option walk, the subcommand check and the operand walk all become correct together."** Both were asserted from the fixes made, not demonstrated against the fixes' own edges. See the entry below for what round 5 found and why the fix this time is a restructuring rather than a fifth instance of the same patch.

- [2026-09-03] **#2245: whether an on-disk path may move is a question about what is written there, not about how visible the path is.** #2231 unified the project-key rule into `store::project_identity` but left `commands::tabs`'s FR-ED-3 writer lease composing its own layout — `tab-session-writers/<key>.lock`, a file *named by* the key, where the transcript log, the adoption log and TD-20's ownership registry all name a directory through `project_dir`. Relaying it moves a path the app writes on disk, so the card was filed rather than taken in #2231 and routed to CJ. Establishing safety took three checks, and the decisive one was not about the path at all. (1) **Is anything durable there?** No: `acquire_writer_lease` opens the file and never reads or writes a byte, and nothing anywhere reads it — its entire state is the `fs4` OS lock held on the open handle. (2) **Does it survive a restart?** No: that lock is per-open-handle and the OS drops it on process exit, crash included, which is exactly why the lease self-heals and needs no liveness check. So it is advisory *within* a session and load-bearing across nothing. (3) **Does one exist?** No: the repo has published no release (only the `v1-legacy` tag), and `%LOCALAPPDATA%/com.cowrite.desktop` on the only real machine holds `EBWebView/` and `transcripts/` with no `tab-session-writers/` at all — every occurrence of that name on this machine is under a test temp directory. A path that stores nothing, survives nothing, and exists nowhere costs nothing to move; a flat `<key>.lock` from some development build would be a zero-byte file, left alone exactly as TD-12's amendment leaves an inert pre-TD-20 `lock*` line alone. This is the #1746 disposition reached by a different route: #1746 could say "`store::lock` has no production caller", whereas the lease *does* have one (`TabbedEditor` → `begin_tab_session`) and is safe anyway, because the caller writes no content. The one residual worth naming is a concurrently running pair of builds straddling the change, which would lock two different paths and both believe they hold the writer lane — a development-only situation for an unshipped app, not a migration question. **The reusable lesson for the guard, not just the finding:** the test that already existed compared the four stores' *key strings*, reading the lease's `file_stem()` and the other three's directory `file_name()` — it varied over the stores and held the shape fixed, so it passed on precisely the divergence it was written to prevent. Pinning the shape as well (`<area>/<key>/`, read off the directory each store itself resolves) is what makes "one rule" mean one rule; the source-text half then has to catch a fifth store that composes its own path instead of joining one through `project_dir`. **And the first draft of that half repeated the very mistake, one level up (round-1 review).** It scanned for the string `project_identity::project_key` and *called itself* "an app-local-data per-project path must be composed by `project_dir`" — but `use super::project_identity::*;` reaches the same function while containing no such string, and the reviewer made `store::adoption` compose its own path exactly that way with the whole lib suite passing 699/0. So does a module alias, and so does a braced import list (`use crate::store::project_identity::{project_key};`) — the very idiom `store::lock` already uses for `project_dir`; a renamed import through the full path is the one escape that needle did catch, which I asserted the opposite of until I ran it. The lesson is the one `test_support.rs` already writes down and I did not take: a source scan is the **weaker** of the two mechanisms and belongs only where privacy cannot express the claim. Here it could — `project_key` is now private with a `#[cfg(test)]` accessor for the two sibling layout tests, so the escape is a compile error rather than a test that has to notice it. Generalizing: when a guard's prose names an axis ("composed by `project_dir`") and its assertion pins a *spelling* ("contains this string"), the gap between them is not a wording problem — it is the guard being built on the wrong mechanism, and no rewording closes it.

- [2026-09-03] **#1827: an event transport is only equivalent when ordering, audience, and resource lifetime survive the substitution together.** The headless driver now retains one append-only, cursor-addressed stream for project-files, delivery, document birth, and the two native File-menu requests. A destructive per-channel drain would let one listener steal another's event; five independent long polls would spend five blocked connections and race project replacement. The frontend therefore uses one synchronously registered multiplexer with one persisted session cursor, ordered dispatch, an in-memory registration-race buffer, and epoch recovery when a restarted driver reports a lower cursor. Project identity is part of the authority boundary: a same-root no-op retains history, successful replace/close advances the epoch and floor, and a direct helper call naming another root may still complete its backend operation but cannot announce into the open project's UI. The root check and append happen under the same mutex—splitting them admitted an outgoing callback between check and push into the successor epoch—and buffered frontend events are likewise cleared on epoch change. One subscriber's exception is isolated from its siblings and later events, and the shared cursor is persisted only after the complete batch dispatch. Long polling leaves only `/events` on a bounded worker lane (eight workers, one-second server and response-write caps); every command remains serial, so a waiter can be woken by a producer without making the command authority concurrent, and eight non-reading clients cannot retain every permit indefinitely. Delivery and birth helpers now publish through the real `McpContext` observer callbacks, while explicit Open/Save-As routes stand in only for the OS menu and dialog-result hooks only for native dialogs. The four browser witnesses assert visible tabs/cards/editor state and the old `notifyDelivery`/`notifyDocumentBirth` participant hooks are deleted. The reusable rule is that a cursor solves replay only after its project epoch, root-scoped producer, single consumer order, cancellation, and bounded blocked-resource story are explicit.

- [2026-09-03] **#1832: a persisted-setting acceptance assertion needs a lifetime boundary, not another read inside the dialog that wrote it.** The Settings surface correctly awaited `setSettings`, updated its committed React state, and announced success; an assertion made before teardown would therefore stay green if the backend write vanished, because the same component already held the selected `2000` value. A full browser reload destroys that draft and every other React-owned value, while the D23 driver process retains its real `store::settings` data directory. Reopening the project and a fresh Settings dialog therefore joins both production participants in one observable: only `setSettings` can put `2000` across the boundary, and only `getSettings` can bring it back. Replacing either call independently yields the same post-reload `Expected: "2000" / Received: "1000"` failure. The reusable rule is that persistence coverage must assert after the caller's in-memory state has ceased to exist; an on-screen “saved” state proves only what the component believes. Because the driver store is process-scoped across this serial suite, the successful case restores `1000` through the user surface after making the load-bearing assertion, rather than bypassing the participants with a cleanup helper.
- [2026-09-03] **#2254: a live preference change must replace the timer, not the editor that owns it.** P2a-9 already read autosave settings inside `EditorShell`'s document-mount effect, so the tempting Settings implementation was to add the settings value to that effect's dependencies. That would make a preference toggle destroy and recreate CodeMirror, taking the document's caret and undo history with it. The Settings surface instead persists the complete fixed settings blob first, then sends only the committed autosave value through the existing owner chain (`App` → every mounted tab → its editor). Each editor cancels the old debounce and replaces it inside the same document lifetime; disabling stops future schedules, and enabling while the buffer is already dirty arms the new interval immediately. The assembled test opens two documents because a one-editor assertion could not distinguish the intended broadcast from an active-tab-only update; the editor-level test separately pins the original CodeMirror instance, selection, and undo depth. A generation guard makes the ordering explicit: a slow initial `getSettings` cannot overwrite a newer user commit. The surface's behavior is shared, but its chrome is not: the dialog and shell trigger stamp one platform fact, and their CSS selectors bind the Windows border/radius family or macOS hairline/radius family plus each exact window shadow from `platform-variants.md`. The reusable rules are that configuration reload and content lifetime are independent identities, and that a shared component still needs an explicit platform seam when its binding appearance differs.
- [2026-09-03] **#1826: replacing an in-process event fake with polling exposes ordering that the fake let the test author choreograph away.** D23 already had the real producer (`RunQueue`) and its `/run_state` projection; the missing participant was the mounted production consumer, so `listenToAgentActivity` now preserves Tauri's push subscription and polls that projection outside Tauri. Three details make the transport equivalent rather than merely sufficient for one happy-path card: it projects the whole queued list, replays an ordered terminal ledger before the successor even when several runs finish between polls, and ends a polling episode at idle/terminal while UI start/stop wakes a new one. A newest-only `last_finished` scalar looked sufficient while every test advanced one completion per sample, but the real queue can stop A, complete B, and start C inside the gap between two 50ms polls; A then vanished and its card remained stuck or absent. Every terminal is now inserted exactly once by stable run id and replayed idempotently, with the full status fingerprint suppressing unchanged frontend deliveries. The ledger is complete for the current open-project lifetime so a remounted subscriber cannot age out an unseen event, then clears atomically on an actual project replace/close; an under-lock same-root recheck is `AlreadyOpen` and must retain history even when a concurrent opener won after the optimistic check. Making the ledger process-global would leak one project's runs into the next, while bounding it without a durable acknowledgement cursor would recreate the loss. The old test hook could deliver a terminal only after a script had advanced the comment clock and persisted `pending_run`; a real spawn failure can beat that bookkeeping. The durable fix is state-derived: TimelinePanel reconsiders an already-rendered terminal whenever its owned thread snapshot changes, guards the conclusion by run id, and timestamps the appended conclusion strictly after the pending event so the `(timestamp, id)` fold cannot reverse causal append order. The acceptance test begins at `[Address with AI]`, observes the real failed run through `/run_state`, proves the sidecar clears, and reopens the UI; removing the subscribe-on-mount caller now breaks the test. The production polling setup also exposed a real-layout race in the existing strict tail-follow scenario: after an arriving card was measured and followed, the outer app could settle 38px shorter without another `VirtualList` render, leaving commit-only container measurement at the old viewport. The list now observes its own resize and re-pins only a reader whose standing choice is still tail; the away-reader guard is unchanged. The transferable rule is that transport substitution must preserve both endpoints and let their natural scheduling race—otherwise a green event test may be proving the script's order, not the product's.
- [2026-09-03] **#2064: provider continuity has two durable layers, and only one may be discarded during recovery.** The rendered D15 transcript is the user-visible conversation; the CLI's provider session id is replaceable machine-local scaffolding beside it. Persisting the latter under the same project key makes close/restart continuity real without putting runtime state in the repo. A stale resume is not inferred from a missing reply or generic provider failure: the unsuccessful result must positively name an unavailable session/conversation, and retry is permitted only before any announced or persisted delivery. The queue then writes an explicit cleared identity before one fresh spawn of the same run/request. This ordering makes a crash safe, bounds replay, and lets the fallback's new init replace the identity. The successful `Recovered` terminal phase exists only to carry the UX fact across the backend/frontend boundary; the timeline renders and persists one ordinary chat notice while leaving the earlier transcript intact. The reusable rule is: visible history and provider continuation are related by project identity, not by failure lifetime—recovery may replace the latter and must never erase the former.
- [2026-09-02] **#1724: the app knew which document was open and spent that knowledge on the protection boundary alone.** `App` computed the active document's identity on every turn and passed it to `start_run` purely as the D21 operation target, so the agent was never told what the user was looking at — CJ's repro was a sermon open and active while "Summarize this message" summarized `CLAUDE.md`. FR-CHAT-4/D30 gives every submitted run a versioned `open_documents` envelope beside FR-CHAT-3's `quoted_selection`. **The design decision worth recording is where it attaches.** Four surfaces submit runs (composer, batch address, timeline comment card, editor-anchored popover) and all four passed `operationTargets` and discarded the identity; composing the envelope at each of them would have made a fifth surface's omission silent and invisible. It is composed instead inside `timeline/agentActivity.ts`'s `startRun` — the one seam all four already pass through — from a **required** fourth argument, so a new surface cannot compile without stating what its turn knows and `NO_OPEN_DOCUMENTS` is an explicit answer rather than a default. **That guarantee stops at the seam, and it is worth being exact about where.** The components above it take `openDocuments` as an *optional* prop defaulting to "nothing open", matching every other optional prop they carry, so a surface rewired without it would still compile and silently ship an empty set. Making the prop required would have forced every unrelated test that mounts `TimelinePanel` — and FR-CHAT-1's pre-document panel, which has no editor beside it — to supply a reader; the chosen mitigation is instead a test per surface, driven through the assembled app, that each one actually supplies the live set. **Two things the requirement foreclosed that the obvious implementation gets wrong.** `unsaved` has two limbs and reading only the first is silent: a document with no file and no saved baseline (D12 untitled/scratch) is unsaved by construction, while its `TabRecord.dirty` is `false` by ratified design — deriving `unsaved` from that flag alone ships an untitled document as `unsaved: false`, drops its `content`, and deletes the only route to it with every test still green. And the reading is deliberately never a live comparison against disk (FR-ED-2/FR-ED-7), so a tab whose file changed underneath it stays `unsaved: false` and the agent reads the fresher file itself. **The load-bearing test is the seam, not the builder.** A unit test of the envelope passes against a caller that never supplies the documents, which is exactly what shipped: coverage is therefore split so `referenceData.test.ts` owns the field rules, `App.test.tsx` proves each of the four real surfaces supplies the live set off the tab layer at submit time, and `TimelinePanel.startRun.test.tsx`/`.batchAddress.test.tsx` drive the real `startRun` against a mocked Tauri boundary to assert the prompt *literally reaching* `start_run`. **One limit of the claim, stated so it is not read as stronger than it is.** "Every submitted run carries `open_documents`" is a property of the *frontend* submission path, not of `start_run` itself: `commands::agent::start_run_on` is also reached directly by the D23 test driver (`crates/test-driver`), which spawns real runs with no envelope. That is right for a driver whose whole purpose is to bypass the UI, but it means the Rust contract remains "an opaque prompt plus D21 targets" — anything that must hold for *every* run, rather than every run the app submits, cannot rely on this layer. Three questions were filed rather than settled: the J# disclosure surface the content clause needs (#2237), whether `content` is size-bounded (#2238 — CJ ruled a cap is a separate decision, so it ships unbounded, which the existing stdin prompt delivery already makes safe against the OS command-line limit), and `quoted_selection`'s missing pathless representation (#2239).
- [2026-09-02] **#1814: two parser facts that only a real transcript could teach.** Both were found by running the actual tools, not by reading their docs. (a) libtest prints a `#[should_panic]` result as `test path::name - should panic ... ok`; a parser anchored on `test <path> ... <outcome>` drops every such test silently, and the matrix would have reported them as never-run. (b) node:test sets `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT` inside each test-file process, and a child `node --test` that inherits it switches to node's internal v8-serializer reporter and ignores `--test-reporter` **entirely — exit code 0, no warning**; a reporter test that spreads `process.env` therefore proves nothing while passing. The same silent-success shape appears a third time on Windows: a bare `C:\…` reporter specifier is rejected by node's ESM loader, but a backslash-relative form loads nothing and still exits 0. Three different ways for "the reporter did not run" to look exactly like "the reporter ran and found nothing" — which is the very failure mode this card exists to eliminate, reappearing one level down in the instrument.
- [2026-08-31] **#2153 review: a finite secret regex is not a trust boundary, and a `ChildProcess` exists before its PID does.** The first diagnostic projection applied several known-token filters to arbitrary exception messages, stacks, and causes, but the redaction helper's own contract says it is Slack-shaped only; an executed publisher persisted an AWS access key, AWS secret, and unlabeled provider credential unchanged. The correction no longer guesses whether exception text is secret: it persists only a lifecycle-selected reason, controlled error/cause types, and allowlisted runner-module stack locations. Direct thrown-error controls plant those credentials plus argv, environment, stdin, stdout, and stderr-shaped payload, then read the durable bytes and refuse every canary. This keeps the record actionable—the real descendant witness names `descendant-survival`, its controlled reason, and `workload-runner.mjs`—without letting attacker-chosen text cross the durable boundary. Separately, Windows `spawn()` can return a real child with `pid === undefined` and emit `ENOENT` asynchronously. Validating the PID before arming `error` produced a misleading `TypeError` and then an uncaught child error; the client now attaches exit/error listeners first, waits one bounded claim-poll interval for that process error, and only then rejects once as infrastructure loss, with no claim, diagnostic, or result.
