Skills are disclosures; workflows are leaf documents. **Generalization: whenever two projections share one underlying namespace, state who owns an identity before promising one selection or active marker.** The same distinction belongs in state and keyboard semantics: independently sourced sections can fail independently while still composing as one navigation widget.

- [2026-08-18] **Reporting a user's request as contradicting ratified decisions is cheaper before implementation than after, and it changed the outcome here (#1720).** CJ's opening ask contradicted J2's “user-authored project material remains eligible,” `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md`'s drawn loose-file row, and the Rust rail intent. Filing it as a filter bug would have silently overturned that rationale. Filing it as a design delta exposed why the rationale failed at real-repo scale and produced a narrower, citable document boundary instead. **The constitution was not an obstacle to the change; reading it made the change explicit enough to review.** The follow-on review then caught two more places prose could lie — an unscoped “never written” would have overridden D3 agent writes, and a visually split rail without partial-failure/keyboard rules would have delegated product behavior to implementation.
- [2026-07-27] **UXR-Q5 attribution is durable while visibility is derived because the editor's decision report carries ids but no source (#695, #798).** Removing attribution on reversal made redo falsely classify a card's own decision as “decided elsewhere”; retaining it and intersecting it with the current decided-id set lets reversal withdraw the claim and redo restore the original transaction's provenance. An explicit later card action can transfer ownership, but a fresh in-editor decision after undo is byte-identical to redo at the `decidedChangeIds` seam and therefore remains indistinguishable until #798 adds origin to the editor→timeline contract.

- [2026-07-26] **A non-colliding legacy id is not itself a fail-closed mechanism, and a document-aware sidecar lookup needs the project root in its type (TD-16, PR #693).** CJ ratified `parse_changes(doc: Option<DocRef { root, path }>, content)` because the recorded `docs.json` → `fold_changes` resolution cannot be reached from a project-relative path alone until TD-17 supplies backend project state; making the pair a struct keeps “both or neither” structural. The review also caught the distinction identity alone does not provide: a legacy card can still pass its document/generation gate, resolve zero ids, and be falsely retired if an empty decision report is collapsed into “no report.” The seam now preserves `[]` as an explicit refusal and uses fallback ids only when no report exists. The same review pinned the sibling CRLF degradation: disk-byte anchors joined against CM6's LF-only reopen text can lose durable identity and authoritative grouping. That remains #447's coordinate-reconciliation work; the current join refuses rather than guesses, and its prose now states the full cost instead of calling the outcome harmless.

- [2026-07-21] **Fixture lesson from #426: a git hook installed as an *untracked* file silently vanishes on the first branch switch — so an integration test must commit `.githooks/` exactly as production does.** A test set up `feature` with `git add .` (sweeping the untracked `.githooks/` into feature's tree), then `git checkout main` removed it — so a later `git commit` on main hit *no hook* and wasn't gated, while `git merge` (run when the dir happened to be present) was. Same repo, opposite results, purely from *when the working tree had the hook*. The gate itself was correct; the fixture lied. Real usage is safe because `.githooks/` is committed and present on every branch/worktree — which is precisely why the fixture must mirror that (commit the hook into the base, scope `git add` to named files). Generalizes: **when testing a mechanism whose presence is state-dependent (a hook, a config file, a checked-out artifact), reproduce how production *keeps* it present, not just that it exists at t=0** — an untracked fixture file is a different experiment than a tracked one.
- [2026-07-20] **When a card settles a siting question, the row it *doesn't* change is the one that breaks — update the census row you're ruling against, not just the five you're ruling for (#459, HX-2 review).** The spec delta moved the intake branch/worktree derivations out of core `workspace.*` into the optional block I (correctly — with no block I nothing ever derives an intake branch, so the keys are dead weight in core), and updated five *other* census rows in the sibling proposal doc to record "settled by HX-2". The intake row was left pointing at the old `workspace.*` siting, so the two documents this card edits contradicted each other on a core-vs-optional-block boundary — precisely the class of question the card existed to settle. The failure mode is specific and worth naming: **rows you're actively changing get re-read; the row that merely becomes stale as a side effect does not.** An overridden decision needs a louder edit than a confirmed one, because a reader reconciling the two docs at extraction time would otherwise provision the wrong shape and only find out at runtime. Rule: after settling a question in doc A, grep doc B for *every* mention of the thing settled, not just the ones already on the diff.
- [2026-07-20] **#410's editor→card reconciliation accumulates in `TimelinePanel`, not `App` — ride the existing per-project reset, don't build a second one.** Wiring `ChangeSetCard.onDecide` to `editor/trackedChanges.ts`'s `decisionsTransaction` needs two directions: card→editor (ids out through `TabbedEditorHandle.decideChanges`, ids back via the existing `onDecide` return-value contract, #349) and editor→card (a decision taken directly in the live editor — toolbar/keyboard/popover — must still retire the card's own pending count, since nothing about those pre-existing entry points calls `onDecide`). The natural-looking design puts the running "ids decided outside any card" set in `App` (it already owns the `TabbedEditor`/`TimelinePanel` boundary) — but `App` never resets any state on a project switch by *design*: the reset happens structurally, via `TimelinePanel key={project}` remounting fresh (#409's own mechanism). A Set accumulated in `App` would survive that remount and leak a previous project's decided ids into a new project's cards that happen to reuse the same numeric ids (ids are per-document, assigned by the backend parse in document order, so collision across projects is the common case, not an edge one). Moved the accumulation into `TimelinePanel` itself instead: `App` only forwards the *latest batch* (`decidedChangeIds?: number[]`, a fresh array per event — the same "identity, not contents, re-triggers the merge" idiom this file's `commentThreads` prop already established) and `TimelinePanel` folds each batch into its own `changeSetDecidedIds` state, which resets for free on the same remount that already resets `cards`. Net effect: zero new reset-path code, because the reset already existed at the right altitude — the bug this avoids is structurally identical to PR #481's `TimelinePanel`-cards-bleed-across-projects finding, just for a different piece of per-project state. **Known, accepted gap, not solved by this shape:** the accumulated set is shared across every change-set card mounted in one `TimelinePanel`, with no per-card document/tab linkage — harmless today because nothing wires a live `propose_changes` delivery into the running app yet (confirmed: #346, the card that would do that, is escalated/critical, and no production code calls `createChangeSetCard`), so every real card in the tree belongs to the one active document by construction. Scoping this to a real per-document key is real follow-on work if/when a delivery seam lands multiple documents' change sets into one panel at once — not attempted here on spec, and not silently promised either.
- [2026-07-20] **A hand-rolled "merge a batch of ids into a Set, skip the render if nothing changed" fold showed up twice in one diff (#410) — extracted rather than left as two copies.** `ChangeSetCard`'s own editor→card reconciliation and `TimelinePanel`'s batch-accumulation effect are different callers solving the literal same problem (a monotonic superset merge that returns the previous reference on a no-op, so a `useState` setter skips the redundant re-render) — not two variations that happen to look similar. Below the codebase's stated Rule of Three, but the /simplify pass judged two *identical* ~12-line blocks worth collapsing anyway since the "wait for three" convention is aimed at premature abstraction over merely-similar code, not at literally-duplicated logic already in the same PR. Landed as `timeline/decidedIds.ts`'s `mergeDecidedIds`, its own small unit suite (reference stability on a no-op merge is the property worth pinning directly, not just inferring from the two callers' own passing tests).
- [2026-07-18] **Protection watchdog + attribution precedence + detect-and-convert (P2a-20, #150, D21, FR-AGENT-2/5, TD §3.5 finding M11).** Built `agent::watchdog` as three composed, exhaustively-tested pieces: (1) `ProtectedSet` — the live D21 set as the *union* of open tabs ∪ operation targets ∪ in-flight-run pins, where **run pins are the mechanism that makes protection survive tab-close mid-run** (close the tab, the run pin still holds it); (2) an in-flight `AttributionLedger` keyed by run, fed the agent's streamed `Write`/`Edit`/`MultiEdit` `file_path` announcements (TD-5) via `announced_write_targets`; (3) `Watchdog::on_write` — the detect-and-convert handler. **Attribution precedence (M11), the load-bearing decision:** on a detected write to a protected doc, a *positively-attributed* write (a still-unmatched in-flight announcement names that same doc — consumed on match, **exactly one announcement attributes exactly one write**) is converted to CriticMarkup tracked changes (diff known-good baseline → raw write, render, attribute to the agent via `changes::AGENT_AUTHOR`, deliver atomically — attribution batch then one atomic doc rename, FR-AGENT-6); an *unattributable* write (no correlated announcement, or a diff that can't round-trip as CriticMarkup) returns `WriteOutcome::ExternalChange` — the FR-ED-7 signal — and is **never converted on a guess**. **Baseline model:** the watchdog snapshots a doc's on-disk content when it *enters* the protected set and updates it to the delivered markup after a conversion; that snapshot is the "known-good" the raw write is diffed against (in the running app the editor buffer is the canonical source; disk-snapshot-on-protect is the self-contained M2a model). **Reuse / anti-drift:** adopted the prior scaffolding that hoisted `AGENT_AUTHOR` + `ChangeType::from(MarkerKind)` into `store::changes` so the two agent-attributed writers (`propose_changes` and the watchdog) can't drift, and extended it by hoisting `now_millis` (the TD-4 timestamp) there too, refactoring `agent::state` onto it. The convert loop is deliberately *not* extracted from `propose_changes` — the two differ structurally (rebase/groups/notes vs. a whole-file diff with none), so Rule-of-Three says wait. **Scoping call (flagged for review):** the card's Goal names an "FS watcher," but the acceptance criteria are all about the protected-set semantics + attribution + convert + no-partial, all delivered and tested here; `watched_paths()` is the exact subscription surface an FS watch consumes. The OS-level notify thread (reusing the proven `store::watch` pattern) is **deferred** to the agent run-loop unit that will actually drive `on_write` from disk events — building it now would be a consumer-less duplicate with a flaky macOS symlink-canonicalization live test (YAGNI). Positive path is proven end-to-end against the real `fake-agent` binary driving the shipped `claude-raw-write-protected` fixture (P0-8, a real disk write + correlated stream); negative path pins never-convert-on-a-guess. **Observed, unrelated:** the frontend `npm test` (vitest) suite is flaky under the full parallel run (5 failures one run, 1 the next, different sets; each implicated file passes 29/29 in isolation) — a pre-existing condition on `main`, orthogonal to this Rust-only change; logged for CJ, not fixed here.
- [2026-07-16] **Tab-session state is machine-local (TD-12 precedent extended), not TD-4-committed (#143, P2a-6).** FR-ED-3's per-project session restore (open tab set, active tab, dirty tabs' unsaved content up to a cap) needed a storage decision TD §3.1 names the module for (`store/`'s "tab-session persistence with per-tab unsaved-content cap") but doesn't itself settle committed-vs-local. Chose machine-local: `.cowrite/tab-session.json`, excluded from commit/sync via the same committed-`.gitignore`-append pattern TD-12 already established for `store::lock`'s runtime files (factored the append logic out to a shared `store::gitignore::ensure_pattern` both modules call, rather than duplicate it) — session state is "which tabs *this window* had open and *my* unsaved scratch text," not TD-4's collaboration data, and committing it would leak a contributor's in-flight text to every collaborator on every save plus spam review history with pure UI churn. Reasoned from precedent rather than a fresh CJ ruling — flagged in the PR description for confirmation; if wrong, only the gitignore pattern moves, not the shape. Cap chosen at 2 MiB/tab (generous — bites only a genuinely oversized in-flight edit, `TabSnapshot::new` degrades over-cap content to "restore from disk" rather than failing the whole session save). **Also scoped:** persistence writes on every tab-set change (open/close/switch/dirty transition) via a `useEffect` keyed on the `tabs` state object — covers the acceptance journey (type once, reopen) but not a still-open, still-typing tab with no further structural event; genuine continuous-typing autosave is P2a-7's explicit territory, not duplicated here. "Reopening a project" is a real remount (a fresh `TabbedEditor`/page load restoring only from what's on disk), not a synthetic in-memory replay — proven in e2e via an actual `page.reload()`, deliberately avoiding a cheaper-but-dishonest simulation. The OS-level "quit with dirty tabs" interception (`stores/appLifecycle.ts`, `getCurrentWindow().onCloseRequested`) follows `editor/ipc.ts`'s existing Tauri/D23-test-driver split and is unit-tested via the same `@tauri-apps/api` mocking convention `ipc.test.ts` uses — real OS-level exercise isn't possible without a live Tauri window, which nothing in this repo's test infra currently drives.
- [2026-07-15] **Container-relative code-fence recognition in the CriticMarkup parser (#187).** Closes the last member of the #38→#75 leak family, the one #75 explicitly deferred and documented as a known limitation: code **fences** were still recognised by *absolute* column (≤3 leading spaces, unchanged since #38), so a fence indented ≥4 absolute columns inside a list item — `- item\n\n    ```\n    {++x++}\n    ``` ` — wasn't treated as code and the literal markup inside it leaked as a real change. Why #75 didn't fix it: making only the **opener** container-relative is worse than the leak — if the closer stays absolute, a ≥4-column closing fence never matches, the "unclosed fence runs to EOF" rule swallows the rest of the document, and real changes are **silently dropped** (the harmful direction, per CJ's #38 ruling). Correct behaviour needs both halves plus the CommonMark item-truncation rule. The fix, on top of #75's container-indent stack: (1) `fence_opener`/`is_fence_closer` now measure indent with `measure_indent` (tab-expanded columns) **relative to the innermost open container** — a fence is recognised at `container + 0..=3` columns, and a "```" indented ≥4 past the container is code content, not a closer (`measure_indent` replaces the old absolute `leading_spaces`, which is deleted); (2) the fence consume loop is container-aware — blank lines stay inside the block, a container-relative closer ends it, but a **non-blank line dedented below the container's content indent truncates the item and the fence with it** (CommonMark item truncation): the zone ends before that line and the main loop *reprocesses* it (popping the container), so markup after the truncated fence still parses instead of running to EOF. Reprocessing (rather than popping inline) is what makes nested truncation fall out for free — a dedent that ends an inner item but stays inside an outer one lands the line as outer-item content on the next pass. (3) `interrupts_paragraph` passes the container to `fence_opener` too, so a fence at ≥4 absolute columns inside a nested item now interrupts the item's paragraph (closing the doubly-nested `- - text` leak); a fence dedented *below* the container returns `None` there → treated as a lazy continuation, which is the benign (parsing) direction. Top-level fences are unchanged: `container == 0` collapses the relative rule to the old absolute one, so an unclosed top-level fence still runs to EOF. **Tests:** 8 new unit tests in both directions (issue example excluded; closed-fence-then-real-markup, dedent-truncation, sibling-dedent truncation, nested container-relative, paragraph-interrupt, over-indented-closer-is-code, top-level regression) + 2 new arms on `prop_marker_excluded_in_zone_but_parsed_outside` (in-item fence excluded; truncation preserves a dedented marker). Full workspace green (Rust 176 + textops 218, JS 22), clippy+fmt clean, matrix check 378/378. Module doc's known-limitations section updated — the absolute-fence bullet is gone; blockquotes and setext/thematic-break boundaries remain the documented simplifications. Pure textops — no constitution delta.
- [2026-07-15] **List-aware indented-code exclusion in the CriticMarkup parser (#75).** Closes the one deliberate leak #38 left open: markup inside CommonMark **indented code blocks** parsed as real changes. The #38 saga established why the naive fix is worse than the leak — an absolute-column rule silently **drops real markup** in indented list-item continuation paragraphs (`1. step\n\n    a {++real++} change` is a paragraph, not code), and dropping a change is the harmful direction. The fix is the minimal block model the issue prescribed: `exclusion_zones` now runs a per-line state machine with (1) a **stack of open list-item content indents** (columns, tab-expanded to 4-column stops), so the code threshold is `container_content_indent + 4`, not 4 absolute; (2) an **open-paragraph flag** — indented code can never interrupt a paragraph (CommonMark), so lazy/continuation lines keep their markup at any indentation; (3) list-marker parsing (`-`/`+`/`*`, 1–9-digit ordered `.`/`)`) with the CommonMark content-indent rules: 1–4 spaces after the marker set W; ≥5 spaces mean the item *starts with indented code on the marker line*; an empty marker is W = marker+1; same-line nested markers (`- - x`) stack; and the paragraph-interruption refinements (empty items and ordered markers ≠1 don't interrupt — except inside an open list, where any sibling/deeper marker starts a new item). ATX headings are recognised as block boundaries (code may open right after, no blank needed). **Deliberate simplifications, all erring toward parsing (the benign direction), documented in the module doc:** fences stay absolute-column (a fence indented ≥4 in a list item isn't excluded — pre-existing #38 behavior, unchanged); blockquotes aren't containers; setext/thematic breaks aren't boundaries. **The interesting fallout was in the *generators*, not the parser.** The P4a atom generators promise "text/content atoms cannot form structure" (that's why their charset has no backticks/`~`/`-`). #75 made **line-start whitespace structural**, and spaces can't leave the charset (whitespace-only markup is first-class — the v1 whitespace-leak fix), so the promise is now enforced positionally instead: `plain()` atoms are letter-leading in all three generator copies (critic/apply/coords), and apply's properties wrap their atom vecs in `atoms()`, which prepends a letter-leading text guard. That guard exists because 20k-case runs found a genuine new phenomenon: `{~~    ~>x~~}{++A++}` — *rejecting* the substitution keeps its all-space `old` side at line start, and the intermediate text `    {++A++}` re-parses with the insertion inside an indented code block; stepwise apply then diverges from batch apply. This is the known "resolving a change reveals structure" class (sibling of `resolving_a_change_may_reveal_marker_shaped_literal_text`), now pinned by `resolving_a_change_may_reindent_a_later_marker_into_code` — P4a convergence holds on structure-stable documents, and the divergence outside that domain is documented behavior, not a bug. Also caught: `PROPTEST_CASES=20000` blows the default global-reject budget (1024) on `prop_assume!`-gated properties — an artifact, not a failure; raise `PROPTEST_MAX_GLOBAL_REJECTS` alongside. **Tests:** 20 new unit tests (both failure directions: exclusion top-level/list/nested/tab/CRLF/marker-line code-start; and the parse-preserving cases — the exact #38 regression, lazy continuations, 4-col list continuation paragraphs, lone-dash and ordered-≠1 non-interruption) + 4 new arms on `prop_marker_excluded_in_zone_but_parsed_outside`; full suite green at 20k property cases. Matrix rows P4a and FR-ED-4 updated; matrix check 341/341.
- [2026-07-12] **Display-mode transforms All/Simple/None/Original (P1-7, #43).** New `textops::display` — the four FR-ED-4 track-changes display modes as pure text projections of raw, on top of P1-3's apply ops and P1-6's `coords::clean`. One public function: `project(raw, mode) -> String`. **Semantics, pulled from the v1 app's `displayModes.ts`/`computeDerivedContent` (legacy/v1, ported as behavior, not code — TD-2 removed the tree-model machinery it was built on):** `All` → `raw` unchanged (the editable source view); `None` → `coords::clean(raw)` (accept-all); `Simple` → the same clean text as `None` — v1's only difference for Simple was margin-bar decorations beside changed lines, which is a rendering concern over the *displayed* editor, not a text projection, and stays out of scope here (lands with the display-mode UI, P2a-8); `Original` → `apply::reject_all(raw)`. **Design call: `Simple`/`None` are kept as distinct enum variants even though they project identical text today** — FR-ED-4 names four display modes and future callers (settings persistence, the toolbar control) need a stable four-way type, not a three-way one with a UI-layer special case; collapsing them now would be the wrong altitude (a type-level shortcut standing in for a decoration difference that hasn't been built yet). **Scope boundary:** this is read-only and whole-document by construction (TD-11 corollary, already named in `apply`'s module doc: "display-mode projections (P1-7)... are batch and safe by construction") — no new coordinate-mapping surface, `coords::clean`/`apply::reject_all` are reused verbatim, not reimplemented. **P1 acceptance, literally:** the issue's acceptance criterion is "P1 no-drift-under-operations property covers mode toggles" — added `prop_mode_toggles_never_drift_the_underlying_document`, which builds a P1-1 `Document`, runs an arbitrary sequence of `project` calls (simulated mode toggles) against it, and asserts the document's text *and* content-hash `Revision` are unchanged afterward — a literal encoding of "a view operation is a read," using the actual `Document`/`Revision` types rather than restating the claim structurally. Plus a cross-check property against the independent P4a anchors (`None`==`accept_all`, `Original`==`reject_all`) so the display-mode match isn't just re-deriving itself. **TDD note:** followed the P1-6 process — stubbed `project` with `unimplemented!()`, wrote the full test suite against the stub, confirmed all 13 new tests failed (red), restored the real three-line match (green). **Simplify pass:** dropped an unused `Hash` derive on `DisplayMode` (inconsistent with the sibling `Decision` enum's derive set, nothing hashes it yet) and a redundant property (`prop_returning_to_all_mode_recovers_raw_byte_identical`) that looped through up to 20 discarded mode toggles before asserting exactly what `prop_all_mode_never_alters_raw` already proves unconditionally — `project` is pure and stateless, so intervening calls can't affect a later one; the loop was wasted computation restating an existing property, not added teeth. **Tests:** 7 unit + 5 proptest properties (12 new, one net simplification removed). 171 workspace tests (up from 159), clippy+fmt clean, test-matrix `matrix:check` green. Matrix **P1** → `present` (the view-operation drift sweep P1-1 deferred to this card); **FR-ED-4** stays `partial` — prev/next navigation and the display-mode UI (P2a-8) are still open.
- [2026-07-11] **Coordinate transforms raw↔clean↔displayed + position mapping (P1-6, #42).** New `textops::coords` — TD-6's three named spaces made concrete on top of P1-2's parser and P1-3's apply ops. **`clean(raw)`** is `apply::accept_all` under its TD-6 name (no new logic — the P4a accept-all anchor *is* `clean`). **`displayed(raw)`** is new: the pending-redline text a reviewer sees before triage (J6 "suggestions capture the displayed text") — unlike `clean`, *both* sides of a change stay visible (a deletion's text remains; a substitution shows `old` immediately followed by `new`, delimiters stripped). **Position mapping — the card's real substance:** `raw_to_clean`/`clean_to_raw` map a byte offset between the two spaces by walking the same accept-resolution edit list `clean` is built from (`apply::resolution`, widened to `pub(crate)` rather than duplicated — one match, two callers) as a list of `Span { source, target }` pairs, then two small mirrored linear scans (`forward`/`backward`) over them. **Design calls:** (1) **The exact/collapsed split is a boundary rule, not a heuristic:** a raw offset not *strictly inside* any marker's `full` range maps `Position::Exact` via cumulative delta (the same "touching, not overlapping" convention `anchor::map_through` already uses); strictly inside one, it collapses to `Position::Collapsed` at the marker's clean-start boundary — total, deterministic, no panics. (2) **The zero-width-target tie, found by property tests, not designed up front.** A dropped edit (a deletion, or a degenerate empty-`new` substitution) has an empty replacement, so its clean span is a single point that is simultaneously its own start *and* end — `raw_to_clean` legitimately maps *both* of the marker's raw boundaries to that one clean point (an unavoidable convergence: no clean byte survives to tell them apart), so `clean_to_raw` can only recover **one** of the two. First attempt picked "collapse to start" (mirroring the strictly-inside policy) and shipped a property test asserting *both* boundaries round-trip — which is provably impossible for a zero-width target, and a second, sharper property (`Del("a"), Text("A")` minimal case) caught that the wrong one was being recovered: real surviving text starting immediately after a deletion is the case anchors actually depend on (P3's stated purpose), and that text's own start offset *is* the marker's raw *end* — so the tie-break was flipped to prefer the marker's end, and the corollary generalizes further: when *several* raw offsets share one clean image (adjacent zero-width markers chain), `clean_to_raw` recovers the *rightmost* one (the greedy scan advances past every zero-width span it's already reached). Pinned with a concrete regression test (`{++ ++}{--A--}`) plus a brute-force property (`prop_clean_to_raw_recovers_the_rightmost_raw_preimage`, independently scanning every offset for the same forward image rather than re-deriving the same formula). **Documented as one named, deliberate exception to the round-trip claim** (module doc + `backward`'s doc comment), not silently patched over. (3) **`clean`/`displayed` are conceptually pure-function wrappers** — no new resolution logic for `clean`, a small new `displayed_resolution` match for `displayed` (old+new concatenation has no existing home). **Simplify pass:** dropped a one-call-site `accepted()` wrapper and an `EditExt` trait that existed only to avoid two inline field accesses — both were ceremony without reuse (Rule of Three not met). **Tests:** 31 unit + 6 proptest properties (512 cases each) covering pure-function determinism, exact-outside-markup round trips (plain text and marker boundaries), the collapse-inside-markup policy, the zero-width tie-break (unit-pinned and property-generalized), and end-of-document consistency against `clean(doc).len()`. Workspace: 130 tests (up from 99), clippy+fmt clean. Matrix **P3** → `present`. Scope note: only raw↔clean position mapping is built (per the card/P3's literal acceptance); `displayed` has no position-mapping API yet — P1-7 (#43) builds the per-display-mode (All/Simple/None/Original) projections next and can extend the same `Span`/`forward`/`backward` machinery if it needs displayed-side offsets. **TDD note:** stubbed all four public functions with `unimplemented!()`, wrote the full test suite against the stubs, confirmed every test failed (red), then restored the real implementation (green) — the P1-3 journal's "wrote tests + implementation together" process lesson, applied.
- [2026-07-11] **Anchor model + exact offset mapping through edits (P1-4, #40).** `textops::anchor` — TD-4's shared anchor shape (`Anchor { offset, length, text_snapshot, context_before, context_after, base_revision }`) plus `Anchor::map_through`, the **exact-mapping** half of P2/D17's two-path contract (TD-6: "exact mapping via stored revision when available, else D17 snapshot+context re-anchor"). **Scope boundary (confirmed against the issue graph):** #40 blocks #41 (P1-5a, SPIKE-4 fuzzy re-anchor algorithm profiling) which blocks #103 (P1-5b, fuzzy re-anchor impl + orphan semantics) — so this unit deliberately stops at the point exact offset arithmetic runs out; fuzzy re-anchor and orphaning are *not* implemented here, `Mapped::Touched` is the seam P1-5b picks up. **Design calls:** (1) **`map_through` consumes a *batch*, not a per-keystroke stream.** `edits: &[Edit]` reuses `apply::Edit` (already a generic `{range, replacement}` — no new near-duplicate type, CLAUDE.md) under the *same* disjoint/same-snapshot contract `apply_edits` uses: all edits measured against the anchor's *current* base text (e.g. one diff between two saved revisions, `textops` P1-8), not each relative to the previous edit's result. Tracking a multi-revision session is a fold the caller does: call `map_through` once per revision transition, feeding each `Mapped::Exact` forward. Chose this over a sequential-shift interpretation because it's what P1-8 (diff→edit-list) and TD-7 (per-edit context validation at delivery) actually produce — one edit-list per revision transition. (2) **Overlap policy, decided explicitly rather than left implicit:** an edit is "before"/"after" the anchor (shifts it / leaves it alone) using half-open-range *touching-is-not-overlapping* — mirrors `apply_edits`'s P4a "adjacent changes meet cleanly." Concretely: an insertion exactly at the anchor's start pushes it forward (left gravity, not absorbed); one exactly at its end doesn't grow it (right gravity). Any edit whose range genuinely intersects `[offset, offset+length)` — whole, partial, or a non-zero edit straddling a zero-length point anchor — returns `Mapped::Touched`. All of this is a single classification per edit (`end <= start` / `start >= end` / else-touched) summed as a net delta, no per-edit shifting loop needed since the batch is one-snapshot-disjoint. (3) **`text_snapshot`/`context_before`/`context_after` are carried through unchanged on a successful exact hop** — by the exact-mapping guarantee the anchored text and its neighbors, by construction, weren't touched, so there's nothing to refresh; `base_revision` is the one field that must advance (offset is meaningless without knowing which text it indexes into, TD-6). Freshening context against live document text is explicitly **not** this card's job — it's SPIKE-4/P1-5a's tuning question. (4) **`CONTEXT_WINDOW = 40` bytes, called out as provisional** in both the module doc and a code comment — no spec'd size exists (TD-4 gives the shape, not a number); P1-5a is expected to tune it from real edit-pattern data. Context capture is char-boundary-safe (hand-rolled `floor_char_boundary`/`ceil_char_boundary`, no unstable-std API) and clamps at document edges. **Tests:** unit coverage per classification (before/after/touched × insertion/deletion/replacement, both anchor boundaries, zero-length anchor, zero-length edit-at-anchor, multi-edit net-delta summation) + two proptest properties — `prop_exact_mapping_survives_arbitrary_untouched_edit_batches` (five-segment document, anchor pinned to the middle segment, edits confined by construction to the other four so overlap is structurally impossible — the issue's literal acceptance criterion, "P2 exact-mapping property green under random edit sequences") and `prop_any_overlapping_edit_in_the_batch_is_touched` (an edit guaranteed to start inside the anchor's span always reports `Touched`). Mutation-tested `map_through`'s boundary check (`<=`→`<`): 4 tests broke as expected, confirming real teeth — also caught a `cargo test` stale-mtime gotcha (`sed -i.bak` + `mv` restore left the restored file's mtime older than the mutated build artifact, so a naive re-run silently reused the stale compiled binary; `touch` before re-running fixed it — worth remembering for any future manual mutation probe). 99 workspace tests, clippy+fmt clean. Matrix **P2** → `partial` (exact-mapping half only; re-anchor/orphan half is #41/#103's). **Independent review pending** (separate sub-agent, per CJ) — not yet run as of this entry.
- [2026-07-10] **CriticMarkup parser + P4a generators + v1 regression corpus (P1-2, #38).** `textops::critic` — `parse(&str) -> Vec<Markup>` over the raw markdown, the second textops unit on the TD-6 substrate. Recognises only the three markers Cowrite persists (`{++ins++}`/`{--del--}`/`{~~old~>new~~}`); CriticMarkup comments/highlights are **not** parsed (Cowrite carries those in the `.comments/` sidecar). **Design calls:** (1) **Byte offsets, not UTF-16 code units.** The v1 parser (legacy/v1 `src/editor/trackChanges/criticMarkup.ts`) indexed by JS string index and split astral chars (`🎉` = surrogate pair → v1 reported 2, we report 4 bytes) — a latent cursor-restore bug. `Markup.full` + inner segment ranges are byte indices into the exact string (TD-6 canonical); regression test `offsets_are_bytes_not_utf16_units_across_astral_chars` pins it. (2) **Whitespace-only markup is first-class — this is the "whitespace-leak" fix.** v1 discarded any marker whose content trimmed to empty (`if (content.trim() === "") continue;`), so an inserted space `{++ ++}` was treated as nonexistent and its literal braces leaked into the clean view (v1 repro: `src/__tests__/tc-accept-edit-repro.test.ts`). Here `{++ ++}` parses as an insertion of `" "`; a *truly* empty `{++++}` still doesn't parse (content must be ≥1 byte — the natural floor via "first closer strictly after content start", no special-case needed). (3) **Uniform `MarkupKind` carrying inner ranges** (`Insertion{text}`, `Deletion{text}`, `Substitution{old,new}`) so P1-3 apply is `accept → keep new/text, reject → keep old` with no re-scan. (4) **Exclusion zones hand-rolled, no regex dep.** The `regex` crate isn't a textops dependency and can't do the exact-N backtick-run match anyway; a byte scanner (frontmatter → line-based fences → per-line inline-code spans) is pure, panic-free, and controllable. Excludes **inline code** beyond the issue's "code-fence + frontmatter" wording — v1 did it, and technical fixtures carry literal braces there. Exclusion decided by a marker's **opening** position (matches v1; markers don't legitimately straddle a fence). **Indented code blocks are NOT excluded — deferred to #75 (see the review saga below).** (5) **First-closer-wins for nesting** (`{++outer {++inner++}` → one insertion, inner opener literal) — the defined non-nesting CriticMarkup semantics, exercised as first-class by the generators. **Deliberately deferred:** git-conflict-marker refusal (v1 bailed on `<<<<<<<`) — that's a store/gitops policy, not the pure parser's concern; noted for the callsite. **Tests:** P4a *parse* generators (`prop_parse_recovers_placed_markup` = exact byte-range recovery over adjacent/whitespace-only content; `prop_wellformed_on_arbitrary_input` + `prop_wellformed_on_delimiter_soup` = no-panic/disjoint/on-boundary over random unicode & raw-delimiter soup; `prop_marker_excluded_in_zone_but_parsed_outside`), a `regression_corpus` module (cursor-restore + whitespace-leak), and ported v1 unit cases. 20k proptest cases green; clippy + fmt clean. Registered against matrix rows **P4a** (partial — accept-all/reject-all *convergence* lands with apply ops in P1-3) and **FR-ED-4** (partial — parse half only). **Note for CJ:** did not add a `fixtures`-crate corpus smoke test (parse every Gutenberg/tech doc) — the arbitrary-input + soup properties already cover robustness, and coupling textops→fixtures is better justified when anchor/fold tests need it (P1-4+). Easy to add if you'd prefer it now. **Independent review (separate sub-agent, per CJ) — the indented-code saga.** Verdict *Needs Minor Revision*, 0 Critical. The one Important finding: markup inside a ≥4-space/tab **indented code block** was parsed as a real change (a leak, same class as the fence exclusion). CJ first chose **fix now**; I added paragraph-interruption-aware indented-code exclusion (a ≥4-column line opens code only after a blank/block-boundary/doc-start via `prev_blank`, so lazy paragraph continuations aren't wrongly excluded) + 6 unit tests. **The re-review caught that this over-corrects:** CommonMark measures the 4-column threshold *relative to the list container*, so absolute-column detection **drops real markup** in indented **list-item continuation paragraphs** (`1. step\n\n    a {++real++} change` is a paragraph, not code) — the *more harmful* direction, and list items are common. Correct detection needs a list-container-indent stack ≈ a CommonMark block model, out of P1-2 scope. **CJ's decision on the tradeoff: revert indented-code exclusion entirely** (back to fence+inline+frontmatter = v1 parity + issue scope), keep the rarer/safer error (a literal example in a *top-level* indented block over-parsing), and **defer list-aware indented-code exclusion to #75** with the limitation documented in the module doc. **Lesson:** when a "correct" fix needs a whole parsing layer to be correct, a partial version can trade a rare benign error for a common harmful one — prefer the documented no-op over a half-model. Reviewer's other notes were confirmed-not-bugs (empty-`new` substitution is intended; open-position fence straddle is the documented v1 rule) or forward-looking heads-ups for P1-3 (zero-length `new` slice) and P1-6 (type `Markup` ranges as raw-coordinate-space once clean/displayed spaces exist).
- [2026-07-10] **Fake-agent misbehavior repertoire (P0-8, #34).** Landed the five required misbehaviors (test-plan §1), each invocable by name from a test, via the extension seam P0-6/P0-7 prepared — new `Step` variants + match arms in `playback::play`, nothing existing touched in spirit (one honest signature change, below). **New primitives:** `EmitRaw(String)` (verbatim bytes, no framing/validation — malformed + interleaved output), `EmitOversized { bytes }` (one ≥`bytes` line, valid assistant/text envelope so *only* length is anomalous — keeps the scenario file tiny vs. a multi-MB literal), `WriteFile { path, contents }` (raw disk write bypassing MCP; **no path confinement** — that guard is the consumer's, test-plan §3.5), `Hang` (parks the thread forever), `Exit { code }` (mid-delivery death). **Design calls:** (1) **`Hang` as a real step, not just "stop advancing."** The P0-6 note said never-terminating = stop sending advance tokens in `Step` pace — but that models the *consumer* declining to drive, a test artifact. A watchdog faces an agent that goes silent *on its own* in `All` pace (the real run mode), so `Hang` blocks autonomously (`loop { thread::park() }` — no wall-clock, no spin). Tested via the spawned binary: read the pre-hang events (blocking = condition-based, no sleep), assert `try_wait()==None` (can't be `Some` — no code path past `Hang`, so no race/flake), then `kill()` = the watchdog. (2) **`play()` now returns `Outcome` (`Completed`/`Exited(u8)`) instead of `()`** — the honest way to carry an `Exit` step's code out to `main` (→ `ExitCode::from(code)`) without `std::process::exit` (which would kill the in-lib test). Existing callers used `.unwrap()`/`.expect()` and ignore the value, so no breakage. (3) **Stale-revision via an injected `RevisionAwareToolServer`**, exactly the "inject your own rejecting server" seam P0-7 flagged. Rejection is a **response value** `{rejected:"stale_revision", current_revision, submitted_revision}` asserted by the step's `expect`, **not** a `ToolError` — in the MCP model a stale call is *answered*, not a transport failure; `propose_changes` is the only tool carrying a revision, so that's the only one checked. Test has teeth: the same scenario against a server whose current revision == the proposed one succeeds (`change_set_id`) → `expect` no longer matches → `UnexpectedResponse`. (4) **Misbehavior scenarios stay out of `EXAMPLE_SCENARIOS`** (the contract-validity sweep) — malformed/oversized are invalid by design; `schema.rs`'s exhaustive `Step` match gained a `panic!` arm asserting example scenarios only ever use `emit`/`mcp_call`. **Gotcha:** `serde_json::Value` sorts object keys on re-serialize (BTreeMap, no `preserve_order`), so emitted event bytes are key-sorted — interleaving assertions must expect sorted keys. Added matrix row `NI-fake-agent-misbehavior`. FR-AGENT-6 (delivery atomicity) stays pending — this builds the *rig* to exercise it; the invariant is enforced/verified at P3-3 with the session manager.
- [2026-07-10] **Fake-agent MCP-call scenarios + validation harness (P0-7, #33).** Added the `mcp_call` step + a stub tool server, exactly the extension seam P0-6 planned. **Design (CJ-confirmed):** (1) the fake reaches the tool server through an **in-process `ToolServer` trait**, not real loopback HTTP — deterministic, zero network (test-plan §1), YAGNI (the real loopback+bearer server is P2a-12, TD-1 §3.2). `StubToolServer` answers every TD-1 §3.2 tool with a **fixed, clockless canned response** so scenarios assert exact round-trips; unknown tools → `ToolError::UnknownTool`. The trait keeps P0-8's stale-revision/rejecting servers injectable without touching playback. (2) MCP-call validation is **gated by the same `validate` flag** as stream events (consistent; lets P0-8 send deliberately-malformed calls with validation off). With validation on, `{name, arguments}` is checked against `Schema::McpToolCall` (strict — our own tools) before dispatch. **Refinements vs the P0-6 note:** the step field is `arguments` (not `params`) to map 1:1 onto the `{name, arguments}` schema payload; `arguments` **defaults to `{}`** (not serde's `Value::Null`) so a no-arg tool like `list_documents` still forms a schema-valid call. **MCP is a side channel:** an `mcp_call` step dispatches to the server and writes **nothing** to the stdout event stream — in production these calls ride loopback HTTP, so stdout stays pure stream-JSON; the binary wires a default `StubToolServer` (nothing else to reach yet). `play()` gained a `&mut dyn ToolServer` param (touched main.rs + the P0-6 tests). No result-schema validation — the tool-*result* framing is SPIKE-1 gap #7, deferred to adapter work; round-trip correctness is asserted by an optional per-step `expect` instead. Renamed `every_example_scenario_emits_only_valid_events` → `every_example_scenario_step_is_contract_valid` (it now validates `mcp_call` payloads against `McpToolCall` too); updated the matrix reference and added row `NI-fake-agent-mcp`.
- [2026-07-09] **P0-4 fixture-corpus reconciliation — two parallel implementations of issue #30.** A wave collision: main's `38e79b0` merged a P0-4 corpus (root `fixtures/`, loader crate, authored-CC0 prose + fidelity edge-cases + a 240 KB perf fixture), while PR #60 independently built a *different* P0-4 (its own crate under `src-tauri/crates/fixtures/corpus/`) using **real Project Gutenberg PD sermons** — the exact Edwards/Wesley/Spurgeon named in test-plan §41 — plus the Rust Book ch. 3.2 (MIT OR Apache-2.0). #60 conflicts with main and can't merge as-is. CJ's call: **graft #60's spec-faithful fixtures onto main's crate, keep both prose sets.** Added the 3 sermons (as `project-gutenberg` source entries, `PD-US`) and the Rust Book (`external-permissive`, first non-CC0 fixture) to main's manifest + `LICENSES.md`; kept main's loader, schema, fidelity/perf fixtures untouched. **Key correction:** main's `LICENSES.md`/manifest claimed the Gutenberg texts were "no longer reliably fetchable" and substituted authored prose — #60 disproves that (fetched via the `gutenberg.pglaf.org` mirror; main host times out on `curl`). Narrative corrected. Main's loader enforces the no-NC/no-SA policy via explicit `share_alike`/`noncommercial` bools per fixture (not SPDX-string parsing), so #60's OR-expression classifier edge case is moot here. Added matrix row `NI-fixture-license` (the guard went load-bearing once real third-party PD/MIT/Apache content entered the corpus). Lesson for parallel issue waves ([[agent-wave-orchestration]]): two agents can both fully implement the same issue against different pre-merge tips — the second PR isn't wrong, it may be *better*; reconcile by merit, don't reflexively close.
- [2026-07-09] **Fake-agent scenario format (P0-6, #32) — the surface P0-7/P0-8 extend.** New standalone workspace crate `src-tauri/crates/fake-agent/` (lib + `fake-agent` bin); it is deliberately NOT a dependency of the app so its deps never ship. A **scenario** is `{ name, description?, steps: [...] }`; `steps` is an ordered list replayed top-to-bottom. `Step` is an **externally-tagged enum** — each step is a single-key object `{"<variant>": <payload>}`. P0-6 ships one variant, `Emit(Value)` = write one stream-JSON event as a JSON line. **How to extend (P0-7/P0-8): add a variant + a match arm in `playback::play`; nothing existing changes.** Unknown step keys are a hard parse error (fail fast — a step this build doesn't know is a version/authoring mismatch, not a skip). Extension notes baked into `scenario.rs` doc comments: P0-7 → `McpCall { tool, params, expect }` against the stub tool server; P0-8 → `EmitRaw(String)` (malformed/oversized bytes), `Hang`, `Exit { code }` (mid-delivery death). **Determinism / no wall-clock sleeps (test-plan §4):** `Pace::All` emits every step back-to-back; `Pace::Step` waits for one advance token (a line on the advance reader = stdin for the binary) *before each step* — the caller is the clock. A **never-terminating stream (P0-8) is just "stop advancing"** — no special code, no timers. EOF on the advance stream ends playback cleanly. Event fidelity lives in the scenario JSON files (shapes copied from SPIKE-1 §5.2), not in a big typed event enum — the stream-JSON grammar is enforced by the **published provider contract**: with `--validate`, playback checks each event against `provider_contract::Schema::ClaudeStreamEvent` (the `docs/formats/provider-contract/` schema P0-5 landed), so the fake and the real adapters validate against the *same* contract — no crate-local copy to drift. Validation defaults off so P0-8 can emit invalid output on purpose. (Original P0-6 shipped a provisional crate-local schema pending P0-5; when P0-5 merged, the code-review re-pointed at `provider-contract` and deleted the copy — [2026-07-09].)
- [2026-07-09] **Provider-contract schemas + validation helper (P0-5, TD-1).** Published five JSON Schemas under `docs/formats/provider-contract/` (Claude + Copilot stream-JSON grammar, MCP server config, permission-hook decision, MCP tool-call payloads) and a reusable `provider-contract` Rust crate that embeds them and validates values/JSONL transcripts. Non-obvious decisions: (1) **Home = a new sibling crate, not the `agent/` module** of the Tauri app crate — the fake agent (P0-6/7/8) is a standalone binary and the §3 integration tests must validate without depending on the `cdylib`/`staticlib` app crate; a small library crate is the only reusable unit that spans binary + test boundaries. Conceptually the contract belongs to `agent/` (tech-design §3.1); flagged for CJ. (2) **Stream-event schemas are deliberately drift-tolerant** — an unknown `type` value validates so a newer CLI build still parses (this IS the test-plan §1 version-drift canary: "a recorded real session still parses under the schema the fake is built from"); only known event types are checked for load-bearing fields. `mcp-tool-call.schema.json` is the opposite — strict `additionalProperties:false`, because those are *our own* tools. (3) **`schema_version` is schema-*document* metadata, not an instance field** — provider stream events are external CLI output we cannot require fields on; the version identifies the contract revision. (4) MCP tool-call argument shapes are grounded in the binding TD-1 §3.2 table; the tool-*call*/*result* stream-event framing was not exercised in SPIKE-1 (gap #7) and is deferred to adapter work, noted in the schema + README rather than fabricated.
- [2025-02-12] Phase 3 chose API-based AI integration (Anthropic Messages API + GitHub Models API) over CLI-based. CJ decided API for both providers. Streaming via Tauri Channels with SSE parsing.
- [2025-02-12] CJ noted we may want to revisit CLI integration (Claude Code CLI, Copilot CLI) for AI chat. The CLI tools have better built-in support for local repo context — they understand git repos, can read files, run tools, and respect project CLAUDE.md natively. The API approach requires us to manually discover and send context. Worth evaluating CLI as an alternative or complement in a future phase.
- [2026-07-09] **SPIKE-2 verdict: GO on `git2` for TD-3/TD-4 — no `gitoxide`/system-git fallback needed** (issue #36, report `docs/spikes/spike2-git2.md`). Verified on macOS with `git2` 0.20.4 / libgit2 1.9.4 (statically vendored). Static TLS is OS-native (macOS SecureTransport, Windows WinHTTP — both from the `libgit2-sys` build script, no OpenSSL for the TLS path); live HTTPS clone, token credentials, worktree create/lock/prune, and shallow clone all pass. **The critical result:** the **union merge driver works natively in libgit2 — no `git_merge_driver_register` (the `git2` crate doesn't expose it anyway)** — verified with a real 3-way merge that conflicts under the default driver and auto-unions with `merge=union` set, control included. **Attribute-mechanism decision: committed `.cowrite/.gitattributes` (`*.jsonl merge=union`)**, not per-clone `.git/info/attributes` — it travels with the repo automatically, fits D1/portability, and sub-directory scoping keeps it off the user's root `.gitattributes` (closes review-finding M5). Three caveats carried to implementation: (1) macOS build **must** add `vendored-openssl` or it links a dev-only homebrew dylib (`https` pulls `openssl-sys` at `cfg(unix)` even though macOS uses SecureTransport) and won't launch on user Macs; (2) `ProxyOptions::auto()` reads only git-config `http.proxy`, not env/WPAD — Cowrite must read OS proxy and pass explicit `url()` (Windows system-proxy pickup UNKNOWN, verify on a Windows box); (3) libgit2 has **no** partial/blob-filter clone (shallow only) and union doesn't dedupe (the `store/` event fold must be idempotent). Windows path is REPORTED (platform-agnostic C, low risk) — run the probe on the P0-2 Windows CI matrix. Spike code throwaway in `/spikes/spike2-git2/` (gitignored), never merges.
- [2026-07-05] **Rewrite planning complete — four documents.** `docs/functional-spec.md` (D1–D23, FR-*), `docs/ux-design.md` (J1–J8), `docs/tech-design.md` (TD-1..8, SPIKE-1..5, build order), `docs/test-plan.md` (P1–P6 properties, fake-agent contract-parity, open-source fixture corpus, spike TDD exemption CJ-approved). Each doc: interview-driven, then fresh-eyes subagent review, findings incorporated. Next step: SPIKE-3 (editor-feel prototype) + SPIKE-1 (Copilot MCP contract) in parallel per the build order.
- [2026-07-04] **Rewrite planned.** CJ decided to step back, nail down functional requirements, and likely rebuild from scratch. Full functional spec built via structured interview (22 decisions D1–D22): see `docs/functional-spec.md`. Headlines: agentic Claude Code + Copilot CLI integration replaces API/prompt-piping (resolves the 2025-02-12 API-vs-CLI tension in favor of CLI/agentic); skills/workflows are repo-owned portable artifacts (`biblical_writing` is the reference repo); agent writes repo freely but protected documents (open tabs ∪ review sets ∪ operation targets) only receive tracked changes; collaboration = GitHub backbone + Cowrite reviewer mode with a worktree-per-review branch model (CJ's idea); reviewers propose edits as suggestion-comments, never file edits; Word/PDF round-trips explicitly rejected.

## Technical Insights

- [2026-11-11] **williacj/emend#58: J4 first-click Working assertions raced D23's run failure.** Under e2e no agent CLI exists, so every single-thread address run fails. The frontend learns this from the `/run_state` poll that `timeline/agentActivity.ts` wakes `POLL_INTERVAL_MS` (50 ms) after `start_run` acknowledges. The `failed` fold then replaces the Working-only line ("Addressing with AI — …") with "Address run failed — nothing proposed for this comment." Three J4 tests (single open thread, re-delegate, defend) asserted that line right after the click, so on a slow runner they read the terminal state instead. A temporary 500 ms wait after the click reproduced the hosted macOS failure text exactly. 90 plain runs under 12 CPU hogs did not, so the window is narrow locally. Fix: the shared `addressWithAIWhileInFlight` helper holds `/run_state` responses (a cadence substitution, test-plan §7b, the same mechanism #2258 already used) while it checks the durable TD-4 `address` event in `you.jsonl` and runs the in-flight assertions. It then releases the poll and waits for the run's durable `failed` `concluded_run`, so later driver writes start from one settled state. Two helper pitfalls: `page.unroute` while a handler is parked on the release promise fails that handler with "Route is already handled!", so release with `unrouteAll({ behavior: "wait" })`. Reading a JSONL file that the backend is appending to can catch a partial last line, so parse only newline-terminated records. With the 500 ms wait in place, the held version passes; removing the hold fails all three with the hosted message. Changing `deriveThreadState` to return `addressed-pending` for a started run fails all three in-flight negatives.
- [2026-11-11] **williacj/emend#59: the #2537 nearby-anchors flake read gutter geometry in the frame between browser layout and CodeMirror's measure.** After `.cm-content` gets `font-size: 24px`, the test polled the paragraph's DOM height until it grew, then read the fourth icon's offset from its `.cm-line` once. The browser lays out the new font synchronously on that read. CodeMirror only updates its height map in its next `requestAnimationFrame` measure. That measure sends a `geometryChanged` update, and only then does the comment gutter rebuild with new `top` values. Because `top` is part of the widget's `eq`, the rebuild also replaces each moved icon's DOM. So between the two steps, the icon still has its pre-font `top` (hosted macOS: 19.75 against ~2). The hosted Windows failure, a null `closest(".cm-line")`, is most likely the same window seen from the DOM side: the `locator.evaluate` resolved the old button, and the rebuild detached it before the evaluate ran. That is inferred from the widget's `eq`; it was not reproduced locally. Wrapping `requestAnimationFrame` in a throwaway probe to defer each frame by 1s reproduced `Received: 19.75` exactly, 3/3. Unamplified, the test passed 30/30 locally. The fix polls the offset with `expect.poll(...).toBeCloseTo(2, 0)`, reading icon and line in one atomic `evaluate` on the editor. With the fix, the same probe passed 3/3. A mutation that skips the gutter rebuild after the font change still fails with `19.75`. No product code changed: a one-frame lag behind the height map is how every CodeMirror widget positioned from the height map behaves. **General rule: a DOM-size condition does not mean CodeMirror has caught up. Poll on the property the test asserts, not on an earlier proxy for it.**
- [2026-11-11] **williacj/emend#57: the #2594 gutter-add flake was a geometry read taken before the comment threads had loaded.** `checkAdd` waited for the selection-time `+` and then measured it against the existing thread's gutter icon with one `Promise.all` of `boundingBox()` calls, without first waiting for that icon. The `+` needs only the live selection, but the existing icon needs `load_comment_threads`, which resolves asynchronously. Until it does, the gutter has no anchor to stack below, so it correctly draws the `+` at `top: 2`. `boundingBox()` returned the `+` at once, then waited for the existing icon to attach, at the same `top: 2`. That gives `add.y == existing.y`, which is exactly the hosted Windows failure (`>= 189.75`, received `167.75`). Delaying only the `load_comment_threads` request by 3s in a throwaway probe reproduced those exact numbers 3/3 on Win32 and 3/3 on MacIntel (`>= 202.75`, received `180.75`). Unamplified, the test passed 60/60 locally. The fix waits for the existing icon to be visible before measuring. Both icons then come from one decoration build, where their 24px offset is invariant. With the fix, the same 3s probe passed 6/6. A mutation that pins the `+` at `top: 2` still fails with the CI numbers. No product code changed: drawing the `+` before threads arrive is correct. **General rule: before measuring the relation between two elements, wait for both of them. A locator read that auto-waits for the second element can return the first one from an earlier layout.**

- [2026-10-02] **#2539 collapsed batch landing test card.** Guarantee: an accepted `Address all…` run started from a collapsed Timeline reveals its owning run card at the feed bottom with 12px clearance and the 4g outer focus ring, while project-open/comment-link navigation keeps its existing J9 top clearance and inset ring. Seam: the production `TimelinePanel` reveal request through measured `VirtualList` geometry and the focused card's computed CSS in the D23 browser. Killer mutation: route the accepted batch through the older J9 top reveal and generic inset focus rule. Oracle: `docs/design/specs/timeline-rail.md` § Run landing (`4g`) compared with `state.md`'s `13h` J9 rule. Axes varied: short and tall owning card and route origin; fixed: file-backed document, light Win32 browser, accepted admission. Substitutions: D23 transport only, real Vite/React/CSS/virtualizer. Selector: `npm run test:e2e -- --grep "#2539 collapsed batch"`; pure geometry selector `npx vitest run src/timeline/virtualization.test.ts -t "#2539"`.
- [2026-10-02] **#2539 measured-growth test card.** Guarantee: while a newly accepted submitted-turn card retains its landing and the author has not scrolled, a later measured row growth keeps its 12px bottom clearance; once the author scrolls away, subsequent growth leaves the reader's position alone. Seam: `VirtualList`'s public scroll request plus measured-row and scroll events. Killer mutation: keep the old one-shot correction or ignore a user scroll while recalculating the bottom request. Oracle: 4g's landing rule plus UXR-Q2/UX Principle 2's reader-controlled scroll. Axes varied: initial measurement, growth, manual scroll, later growth; fixed: one active request and a 300px viewport. Substitutions: jsdom's DOM measurements only; production list, effects, and event handler remain. Selector: `npx vitest run src/timeline/VirtualList.test.tsx -t "#2539"`.
- [2026-10-02] **#2539 existing-feature result.** The shipped collapsed `Address all…` route already expanded Timeline, preserved a containing Chat facet or selected All, focused the accepted run, and captured the clean/dirty/over-bound J13 line. Its shared J9 navigation presented the card near the top with an inset ring, contrary to 4g's submitted-turn bottom landing and outer ring. The pure layout test failed RED on both fitting and tall geometry; the D23 browser failed RED with a 230px bottom gap. The first 4g wiring made the ring correct but exposed one-shot measured correction when live run content grew; a focused browser failure measured the card below the viewport. A request-specific measured follow now keeps the 12px bottom clearance until a real reader scroll, leaving J9 requests on their original path. Final: frontend 2,293/2,293; scripts 414 pass/3 Windows-only skip; production build; matrix 3,952/3,952 named definitions; browser 144 pass/2 Windows-only skip; diff check. File-backed selection `Ask agent` remains an unbuilt action, so this existing-feature card makes no claim for that #2539 acceptance limb.

- [2026-09-30] **P4-2 optional Git foundation (D12, TD-3, J5):** `gitops::repository` now distinguishes a plain project folder from one inside a Git working tree, including an ancestor repository, and initializes only on an explicit call. Real-repository tests verify an existing Markdown file survives init, a nested folder does not acquire its own `.git` when the parent is already versioned, and a bare repository fails closed because it has no working tree. `git2` 0.20.4 resolved `libgit2-sys` 0.18.8/libgit2 1.9.7; SPIKE-2 proved union-merge on libgit2 1.9.4, so P4-5 must recheck that property against the selected 1.9.7 before relying on it. The Cargo manifest vendors HTTPS/OpenSSL as SPIKE-2 requires for macOS packaging. The initial sandboxed `cargo test` baseline had existing loopback-bind and native-watch permission failures; the same unchanged baseline passed with host permissions (808 unit tests passed, 3 ignored, and all integration suites green). Neither checkpointing nor GitHub connectivity is claimed here.

- [2026-09-07] **#2396: injected MCP configuration does not establish a usable binding.** The provider may accept its argv yet advertise Cowrite as failed in `system/init`; the session now rejects that inventory through its existing read-error path before continuing the run. The queue already owns attributed `Failed`, child stop/reap, watchdog end, and advancement, so no new UI state or retry mechanism is needed. Only the exact Cowrite server name matters; unrelated failures do not reject a connected Cowrite binding. The provider schema makes the entire inventory optional, so complete omission remains tolerated, while a present inventory must establish connected Cowrite entries. A real provider-child stand-in derives status from `HttpToolServer` initialize/initialized/tools-list against the production open-project server; invalid bearer authentication really returns HTTP401, and a hung failed child must be terminated before the queued valid handshake and document read complete. Diagnostics are fixed text so arbitrary inventory fields cannot disclose credentials. (D21, TD-1/TD-5, FR-X-3, J7/UXR-Q20.3)

- [2026-09-07] **#2419: a socket write timeout is not a response deadline.** The D23 event driver gave each blocking socket write one second, but a macOS loopback probe with a non-reading peer accepted 326,656 bytes over repeated full/partial sends before returning `EAGAIN` at 3.985 seconds; one partial send alone took 1.308 seconds. The eight-client baseline therefore sat near its four-second test bound and sometimes retained permits past it. A continuously reading peer could extend that lifetime indefinitely. The response writer now uses nonblocking Mio I/O and one monotonic deadline across headers, body, partial writes, and bounded writable-readiness waits. It checks the deadline even while writes keep succeeding, and the permit stays owned until the response writer exits. The real eight-client and continuous-reader regressions both fail the old path at a two-second observation bound and pass with the existing one-second contract; a normal reader pins the HTTP status/content length and exact body. This changes neither worker admission nor the serial command lane. Mio was already locked transitively; the driver now depends on its socket/readiness API directly.

- [2026-09-07] **#2415: closing one descriptor is not the end of an OS lease.** The tab writer could be logically removed while `flock` remained held through another reference to its open-file description. Keeping a real `File::try_clone` alive made both `end_tab_session_on` and coordinator teardown fail deterministically with `WriterBusy`; a separate macOS fork probe confirmed the inherited-descriptor mechanism too. `ActiveWriter::drop` now explicitly unlocks before closing its handle. Tests retain exclusion while the owner lives and after the successor acquires, and the existing killed-child test keeps crash release covered. The specific child responsible for the original concurrent-suite failures was not identified; the deterministic regression establishes the descriptor-retention mechanism without claiming that trace. (FR-ED-3, `docs/formats/tab-session.md` writer authority)

- [2026-09-06] **#2390: test the MCP client lifecycle, not only loopback tool calls.** The official `rmcp` client reproduced `-32601 method not found: initialize` against the original server despite the workspace and e2e baselines passing. The server now negotiates initialization, accepts notifications with empty HTTP 202, publishes tool input schemas from the dispatch contract, and wraps results as MCP text plus structured content. The permanent SDK test discovers and invokes all six implemented tools; the fake HTTP provider now initializes and discovers before any playback. Optional standalone SSE is declined with the protocol's HTTP 405 response. The seventh published tool, `permission_request`, still returns Unsupported: the separate P3-2 permission broker and J7 UI are absent, so successful MCP connection alone must not be reported as complete permission-flow support. (TD-1, D21, test-plan §1/§3)


- [2026-09-03] **#2278: three crossed review comments in one night had ONE cause — two reviewer agents writing the same well-known filename in the one scratchpad their session shared — and the "truncated" one was not a partial write but a competing truncating one.** CJ filed it as an investigation item precisely because the shapes looked different: PR #2229's round-1 comment carried an extra block from PR #2231's review (additive), PR #2271's carried ~50 lines of another card's report spliced between its `### Critical` and its `### Important` (additive), and PR #2270's was a byte-identical 93-line prefix of PR #2271's, headed by #2271's title and SHA, with **no `### Important` at all** — the gating Important for #2245 existed nowhere on the PR, surviving only because the coordinating session had relayed it. The mechanism is recorded verbatim in the two reviewers' own subagent transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-williacj-github-cowrite/e1682d29-…/subagents/`): both `abfa5b41…` (reviewing PR #2270) and `a7fea397…` (reviewing PR #2271) wrote **`<session-scratchpad>/report.md`** — 18 subagents of that one session used that exact path. Timeline: `cat >` 23:02:22 (#2245's head) → `cat >` 23:03:18 (#2230's head, destroying it) → `cat >>` 23:03:33 (#2245's chunk onto the wrong head) → `cat >` 23:03:58 (destroying that too) → `cat >>` 23:04:04 (#2245's tail) → **`gh pr comment 2270 --body-file report.md`** 23:04:23 → `cat >>` 23:04:25 and 23:04:57 (#2230's tail) → **`gh pr comment 2271 --body-file report.md`** 23:05:00. Replaying that op sequence against a real filesystem reproduces all four observed properties exactly. **The lesson that generalises past this card:** a *destroyed* section and a *duplicated* one are the same defect seen from the two ends of one interleaving, so "these look like different failures" was the wrong inference — the loser of a truncation race publishes a prefix, the winner publishes a splice. **Second lesson, on building the guard:** the first version was written against the three incident bodies, and a sweep of the 40 most recent PRs flagged nine — six of them *healthy* reports. Three separate guard defects came out of successively wider sweeps, none of which a fixture would have shown: reviewers legitimately spell their sections `- Critical: none` or `**Critical:** 0` under one `### Findings` heading; a re-review legitimately repeats `### Critical` under `## Round 2` and `## Round 1`; and a title legitimately backticks its SHA. The final form flags exactly 3 of 80 — the three known occurrences and nothing else. A guard tuned only on its motivating instances passes on those instances and ships the class. Sibling of #2277 (shared scratchpad crossing an adjudication declaration) and #2268 (`git stash` is repository-wide): the same family — concurrent agents sharing mutable state through a well-known path, failing silently and cross-card.
- [2026-09-03] **#2228: a spec that names a thing the system has no name for is not an implementation gap — it is a copy decision, and an Engineer who guesses it launders a UX ruling.** `docs/ux-design.md` §2's acknowledgment ladder was written as `⏳ Queued behind sermon-review` / `In sermon-review` / `[Stop sermon-review]`, against a run *display name* that was never built — and not merely unplumbed: `start_run` takes no name and no run-carrying wire shape (`RunRequest`, `RunStatus`, `QueuedRun`, `StartRunResult`, `ActivityLine`) holds one, so there was nothing upstream to thread. #1778/PR #2184 hit exactly this and made the right call: it shipped the ladder's *withdrawing* half (a batch-targeted thread stops claiming *Working* and stops offering `[Address with AI]`) and left the rung line unrendered rather than word it, recording the residual in five places. The cost of that correctness was one whole card, and the ordinary J4 batch path was silent on `main` for the interval — which §2 forbids twice, in the negative. **The lesson is about which half is expensive.** Building the rung was a couple of hours; deciding its five words was CJ's, and no amount of engineering care substitutes. So the useful move for the *next* one is to raise the copy question the moment the naming gap is proved, in parallel with the code, rather than as the finding of a completed card. Two things made the gap provable rather than arguable: the only `displayName` in the app is `providerMeta.ts`'s ("Claude Code" — a **provider**), and `skill`/`workflow_run` are agent-supplied strings written at thread birth naming the review pass that *raised* the comment, so rendering either would name the **wrong** run — a confidently-wrong label being strictly worse than the silence it replaced. Recording that second point is what stopped the reword from being re-litigated as "surely something is to hand."

- [2026-09-03] **#2228: "a rung is never traded down" is a rule about the direction a signal fails in, and it decides the data shape, not just the copy.** §2's ladder distinguishes rung 1 (*queued behind an active run*) from rung 2 (*live, this thread targeted but not started, or per-thread progress unobservable*) by one fact `CommentThread` cannot answer. The authoritative answer is `RunQueueState.queued`, and a sweep of `src/` found **nobody holds it** — no `useState<RunQueueState>`, no polling, and the one `getRunState()` call a one-shot inside `TabbedEditor`'s close guard that reads `active` and discards the rest. The tempting shape is `activeRunId` plus `pending_run !== activeRunId`, and it is wrong in a way that only shows up under D23: `startRun` degrades to `{ id: "", queuedPosition: null }` outside a Tauri webview and `resolvedRunId` then mints a client-side id the backend queue has never heard of, so *absence* from the queue's answer means **unknown** — a third answer, not "queued". Deriving rung 1 from a negative therefore claims "queued behind" (i.e. *this run has not started*) about a run that may well be working, on stale or absent data. Reading it from **positive** queue membership instead makes unknown degrade to rung 2, whose line ("it already has this comment as written") stays true either way because it asserts only *that* a run holds the comment. **The generalizable bit:** where two presentations differ by a claim one of them makes and the other does not, derive the claiming one from positive evidence, so every failure of the signal lands on the humbler rendering. §2 had already said this as prose — the ladder "only ever **gains** information" — and the prose turned out to be a specification of the predicate's shape.

- [2026-09-03] **#2228: a status line gated on which state *name* a card displays will vanish exactly where precedence reorders — and §2 said so in a sentence nobody had implemented.** `CommentThreadCard` rendered rung 3's active-run line on `resolvedState === "working"`. But `deriveThreadState`'s precedence puts `orphaned` above `working`, so a thread whose anchor drifted mid-run displayed *Orphaned* and lost the run acknowledgment entirely — while §2's own "The active-run status is not governed by state precedence" says the card keeps that line "regardless of which name it displays", and its exit table spells the case out ("an anchor lost mid-run … keeps the active-run status line"). The defect was invisible for two reasons worth separating. First, **it was one `&&` away from a rule the document states literally**, and a reader checking the code against the *state table* rather than against that sentence sees nothing wrong. Second, it was only forced into view by building rungs 1-2 correctly: those read off `pending_run`/`pending_run_started` rather than `resolvedState`, so they survive orphaning — leaving an asymmetry where the *lower* rungs were more robust than the highest one, which is the kind of thing that reads as deliberate once it ships. **The reusable check:** when a card carries both a *name* chosen by precedence and a *status* that is true independently, they are two reads of the thread and must not share a predicate. Deriving both from one `resolvedState` is the bug, and the tell is a precedence order in the same document as a "regardless of precedence" clause.

- [2026-09-02] **#2231: `OsStr::as_encoded_bytes()` is injective but is not a durable on-disk encoding, and `to_string_lossy()` is durable but not injective — a path used as a storage key needs an encoding you state yourself.** Four app-local-data stores claimed to key by "blake3(canonical root)"; two implementations hashed different bytes (`store::transcript::project_key` the lossy string, `commands::tabs` the encoded `OsStr`), so unifying them forced the byte-level question neither had answered. Both obvious answers fail, in opposite directions. Lossy loses information: every non-Unicode unit becomes `U+FFFD`, so two genuinely distinct roots hash to one key — verified by construction, not argued (`a != b` while `a.to_string_lossy() == b.to_string_lossy()`). Two projects sharing one D15 transcript log is a worse outcome than any orphaning. `as_encoded_bytes()` loses durability: `std` says its non-UTF-8 sub-slices are "only comparable within the same Rust version built for the same target platform" and that storing them in a file "will likely result in incompatible byte slices" — which is the *same* objection `project_key`'s own doc comment had already recorded, in 2026-07-16, for rejecting `std`'s `DefaultHasher` in favour of blake3. The tree had therefore reintroduced by hand the exact hazard one of its own comments warned about, one module over. The general shape: an encoding chosen for *interop with std* and an encoding chosen for *durability on disk* are different requirements, and `AsRef<[u8]>`-shaped convenience on a path silently answers the first. **What made the fix free** is worth recording separately: the two spellings are byte-identical for every valid-Unicode path, so a rule that hashes UTF-8 bytes reproduces every key both old implementations already wrote, and no shipped store moved. That is checkable rather than assertable — re-derive both old spellings *outside* the new rule and assert equality — and it is what turned an on-disk-contract change into an ordinary refactor. **On testing a latent divergence:** every per-store assertion of the form "store X keys root R to K" passed before the fix and would have proved nothing. The assertion that bites compares the keys the stores *actually resolve* against each other, on the one input class where the two rules disagree.
- [2026-09-02] **#1777: "short-circuit" and "stay silent" are two decisions, and a test that only pins the first reproduces the defect.** #1721/PR #1760 wired both single-thread `[Address with AI]` surfaces to `start_run` and correctly refused to stamp a thread pending when the call rejected — then returned from the `catch` with nothing reported, so the user clicked a button and got nothing, no reason given (FR-X-5 MUST, UX Principle 7). Its own test (`never calls addressWithAI when start_run itself rejects`) passes on both the defective and the fixed code, because it asserts the half that was right; the review that caught the missing half read the code, not the suite. The general shape: an error path has an *abort* obligation and a *report* obligation, and a negative-only assertion (`not.toHaveBeenCalled`) can only ever witness the first. Pinning the second needs a test at the altitude where something actually renders — here `TimelinePanel.startRun.test.tsx` (a real `CommentCard` through the real `startRun`) and `App.test.tsx` (a real comment-highlight deep-link into the popover and the whole `CommentPopover`→`EditorShell`→`TabbedEditor`→`App`→`TimelinePanel` wire). All four new report-half tests were confirmed RED against the pre-fix code before the fix was written. **On mechanism**, the issue's stated reason for deferral (`TimelinePanel` owns `failedRunStatus`/`foldRunStatus`; the comment surfaces have no channel to reach them) turned out to need *two* channels, not one, and that asymmetry is a property of the component tree rather than an inconsistency: `TimelinePanel` renders `CommentCard` directly, so a prop suffices, while `CommentPopover` lives under `TabbedEditor`/`EditorShell` and its nearest common owner with the timeline is `App` — which already has exactly this seam (`incomingCards`, the route every card no producer inside the panel can mint already takes). What keeps that from becoming two error presentations is moving `failedRunStatus` out of `TimelinePanel`'s module scope and beside `startRun` in `timeline/agentActivity.ts`, so all three callers build the identical card from one builder. **The thing worth not getting wrong**: no thread state is touched on this path. ux-design.md §2's exit table gives a failed run a thread note (`Address run failed — nothing proposed for this comment.`), but its own rule scopes that to "where the user was shown work in progress" — a run that never started showed nothing, so the thread owes no note, keeps its live `[Address with AI]`, and only the run card carries the reason.
